id: "70255183"
title: "Peace negotiations in the Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)"
---

# Peace negotiations in the Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)

This article is about peace negotiations since the February 2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine. For the previous peace process, see [Minsk agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minsk_agreements).

There have been several rounds of [peace talks](24903.md) to end the [ongoing Russo-Ukrainian war](70149799.md) since it began with [Russia's invasion](79400268.md) in February 2022. These negotiations follow the [Minsk agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minsk_agreements) during the [War in Donbas (2014–2022)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Donbas). Russia's president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) seeks recognition of all [occupied land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian-occupied_territories_of_Ukraine) as Russian, for Russia to be given all of [the regions it claims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts) but does not fully occupy, guarantees that Ukraine will [never join NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_opposition_to_Ukrainian_NATO_membership), curtailment of Ukraine's military, and the lifting of [sanctions against Russia](70323386.md). Ukraine's president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) seeks a [ceasefire](592591.md), full withdrawal of Russian troops, the return of prisoners and [kidnapped Ukrainian children](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_abductions_in_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War), prosecution of Russian leaders for [war crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_crimes_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine), and security guarantees to prevent further Russian aggression.

The first meeting between Russian and Ukrainian officials was held four days after the invasion began, on 28 February 2022, in [Belarus](3457.md), and ended without result. Later rounds of talks took place in March 2022 on the [Belarus–Ukraine border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belarus%E2%80%93Ukraine_border) and in [Antalya](361527.md), Turkey. Negotiations in Turkey proposed that Ukraine would abandon plans to join NATO and have limits placed on its military, while having security guarantees from Western countries, and not being required to recognize Russia's [annexation of Crimea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annexation_of_Crimea_by_the_Russian_Federation). Negotiations halted due to disagreements over key points, Russia's proposed veto on Ukraine's defense, doubts about Russia's sincerity, and the [Bucha massacre](70455343.md). The negotiations did not deal with borders or territory. In November 2022, Ukraine presented a [10-point peace plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine's_Peace_Formula), which was rejected by Russia.

Renewed negotiations began in 2025 after [Donald Trump](4848272.md) became president of the United States. [The Trump administration's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Presidency_of_Donald_Trump) formulated peace plans have been generally favorable towards Russia, but they have been rejected by both Russia and Ukraine. European countries have been more aligned with Ukrainian proposals, and have planned for a ceasefire guarded by a "[coalition of the willing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coalition_of_the_willing_\(Russo-Ukrainian_war\))" with [troops in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proposed_multinational_force_in_Ukraine). Since March 2025, Ukrainian president Zelenskyy has been proposing an unlimited unconditional [ceasefire](592591.md). Russian president Putin has refused these calls for a ceasefire, saying Russia will continue its war until its demands are met.

## Background

Russia, the United States and the United Kingdom are signatories to the 1994 [Budapest Memorandum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budapest_Memorandum) political agreement, providing security assurances to Ukraine. China and France gave somewhat weaker individual assurances in separate documents. Russia reaffirmed this assurance in 2009.[^cite_note-cfrBudapest-1] After the [Russian occupation of Crimea](71944314.md) began in 2014, Putin claimed that the [Revolution of Dignity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolution_of_Dignity) had created a new political entity and any prior agreement with Ukraine was therefore void.[^cite_note-guardianClintonBudapest-2]

Since the [dissolution of the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union), Russia has repeatedly engineered [frozen conflicts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frozen_conflicts) to block the expansion of [NATO](21133.md) or the [European Union](9317.md) into the former Soviet sphere of influence.[^cite_note-palladiumFrozen-3][^cite_note-fpFrozen-4][^cite_note-perFrozen-5][^cite_note-grossmanFrozen-6] Conflicts are deemed "frozen" when fighting has stopped but no political resolution has been achieved. In 2014, Russia orchestrated separatist movements in Ukraine,[^cite_note-randLessons-7] and Russian proxy forces began the [Donbas war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donbas_war).[^cite_note-palladiumFrozen-3][^cite_note-grossmanFrozen-6][^cite_note-bbc220526-8] After Ukraine recovered significant territory up to August 2014, Russia intervened with conventional forces, leading to negotiated ceasefires through the [Minsk agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minsk_agreements) of 2014 and 2015.[^cite_note-randLessons-7] These ceasefires had potential to freeze the conflict, but sporadic offensives by Russia-backed separatists continued.[^cite_note-randLessons-7] Although Russia was a party to the Minsk agreements, it later denied any obligations, claiming to have only been a mediator between Ukraine and separatist forces.[^cite_note-atlanticDenials-9] In total, according to military analysts, Russia has broken 190 agreements signed with Ukraine and the international community.[^cite_note-10][^cite_note-11]

In the [lead-up to the invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prelude_to_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine), Russia's president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) repeatedly attacked Ukraine's [right to exist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_to_exist) and referred to Ukraine as "historically Russian lands". In his July 2021 essay "[On the Historical Unity of Russians and Ukrainians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/On_the_Historical_Unity_of_Russians_and_Ukrainians)", he claimed there is "no historical basis" for the "idea of Ukrainian people as a nation separate from the Russians".[^cite_note-Duben-12] On 24 February 2022, Russia began [its invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) as an escalation of the ongoing [Russo-Ukrainian War](42085878.md).[^cite_note-13]

## Negotiating positions

### Russia



Russia's president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md), Foreign Minister [Sergey Lavrov](541346.md), and Kremlin Spokesman [Dmitry Peskov](9214683.md)

Putin outlined Russia's terms for a ceasefire and negotiations in June 2024. He said that Russia must be allowed to keep [all the land it occupies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian-occupied_territories_of_Ukraine), and be handed all of the [provinces that it claims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts) but does not fully control. He also said that Ukraine must officially end its [plans to join NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine%E2%80%93NATO_relations). Further, he demanded that the international community recognize Russia's annexations and lift their [sanctions against it](70323386.md).[^cite_note-Putin1803657-14]

Shortly before the invasion, Russia demanded an international treaty to bar Ukraine from ever joining [NATO](21133.md) but also withdrawal of NATO forces from its own eastern member states.[^cite_note-15][^cite_note-reuters220930-16] This was rejected by NATO as it would go against its "open-door" policy and the principle of [self-determination](29269.md). NATO offered to improve communication with Russia and discuss missile placements and military exercises, as long as Russia withdrew troops from Ukraine's borders.[^cite_note-17]

Russia's demands at the start of the invasion included recognition of [Russia's annexation of Crimea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annexation_of_Crimea_by_the_Russian_Federation), recognition of the [Donetsk People's Republic](42425747.md) and [Luhansk People's Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhansk_People's_Republic) as independent states, as well as "[demilitarization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demilitarization)" and "[denazification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denazification)" of Ukraine, but did not clearly say what these terms meant.[^cite_note-abcau220304-18] Russian propaganda falsely claimed that [Ukraine's government were neo-Nazis](70144390.md) carrying out ["genocide" in the Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accusations_of_genocide_in_Donbas).[^cite_note-nytDenazification-19][^cite_note-cbcErase-20] An editorial "[What Russia Should Do with Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/What_Russia_Should_Do_with_Ukraine)", published in Russian [state media](20803507.md), explained "denazification" as eradication of Ukrainian national identity.[^cite_note-cbcErase-20] Genocide scholar [Eugene Finkel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugene_Finkel) said the document was an admission of [intent to commit genocide against Ukrainians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_of_Ukrainians_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-cbcErase-20] It is unclear to what extent the editorial reflected official policy, but Ukrainian-Canadian diplomat [Roman Waschuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Waschuk) said that the appearance of the editorial around the same time as the [Bucha massacre](70455343.md) made negotiations much harder.[^cite_note-cbcErase-20]

In September 2022, [Reuters](18998750.md) reported that Putin's envoy on Ukraine [Dmitry Kozak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Kozak) had struck a provisional deal that would satisfy Russia's demand for Ukraine to stay out of NATO, but the plan was rejected by Putin who preferred a full-scale military invasion.[^cite_note-Reuters14092022-21] After Russia declared it had [annexed four regions of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts), Kremlin spokesman [Dmitry Peskov](9214683.md) said that these additional annexations must be recognized before any negotiations.[^cite_note-voa221230-22] In April 2023, Russian Foreign Minister [Sergei Lavrov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergei_Lavrov) said that he wanted any negotiations to focus on creating a "new world order" to counter the hegemony of the United States.[^cite_note-moscow230507-23] In January 2024, Putin again made statements which suggested, according to the [Institute for the Study of War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_the_Study_of_War), that his "maximalist objectives in Ukraine" remained unchanged, "which are tantamount to full Ukrainian and Western surrender". He again called for the overthrow of the Ukrainian government.[^cite_note-bcfa1-24]

In June 2025, Putin declared that Russians and Ukrainians are "[one people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-Russian_nation)" and therefore, "all of Ukraine is ours".[^cite_note-25]

### Ukraine

Main article: [Ukraine's Peace Formula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine's_Peace_Formula)



Ukraine's president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md), Foreign Minister [Dmytro Kuleba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmytro_Kuleba) (R), and Head of the Presidential Office, [Andriy Yermak](63086056.md) (L)

Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) proposed in November 2022 a 10-point peace plan,[^cite_note-kp10point-26][^cite_note-ajz1-27][^cite_note-28] consisting of:

- A ceasefire and withdrawal of Russian military forces from Ukraine

- Restoration of Ukraine's borders prior to the 2014 annexation of Crimea

- Release of prisoners and return of [Ukrainian children deported to Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_abductions_in_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War)

- Protection and restoration of [Ukraine's energy infrastructure](71963495.md)

- Protection of [food distribution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022%E2%80%932023_food_crises#Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine)

- Nuclear safety, including transfer of [Zaporizhzhia nuclear power plant](71700643.md) to the [IAEA](14984.md)

- Environmental safety, including [demining](2206580.md) and remediation of ecological damage caused by the [destruction of the Kakhovka Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destruction_of_the_Kakhovka_Dam)

- Prosecution of [Russian war crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_crimes_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine)

- Security guarantees for Ukraine to prevent future aggression

- A multilateral peace conference and peace treaty

Two months before the invasion, Ukrainian foreign minister [Dmytro Kuleba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmytro_Kuleba) dismissed the idea that declaring [neutrality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neutral_country) would prevent Russian aggression. He noted that Ukraine was already a neutral country in 2014 when Russia [occupied Crimea](71944314.md) and [invaded the Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Donbas#August_2014_invasion_by_Russian_forces).[^cite_note-29] In negotiations during March and April 2022, Ukraine was willing to consider a neutral status—wherein Ukraine would not join any military alliances or host forces of other nations on its territory—if it received [security guarantees](4991894.md) from its European allies.[^cite_note-reuters220329-30] Reacting to Russian attacks on civilian targets later that year, Kuleba said, "There can be no 'neutrality' in the face of such mass war crimes."[^cite_note-voa221230-22]

During the early negotiations in March 2022, Ukrainian president Zelenskyy said there could be no peace deal without a [ceasefire](592591.md) and Russian troop withdrawal.[^cite_note-31] In August 2022, Zelenskyy said that he would not agree to a ceasefire that would [freeze the conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frozen_conflict) indefinitely with Russia occupying 22% of Ukraine: "We explained that there will be no Minsk-3, Minsk-5, or Minsk-7. We will not play these games, we have lost part of our territories this way ... it is a trap".[^cite_note-guardian220823-32] In September 2022, after Russia proclaimed it had annexed large swathes of Ukraine, Zelenskyy signed NATO application papers.[^cite_note-reuters220930-16] Ukraine has sought security guarantees in the meantime, but there is no consensus within NATO about such an arrangement.[^cite_note-politico230608-33] In June 2023, Zelenskyy said that Ukraine was not ready to negotiate with Russian representatives unless Russia withdrew its troops.[^cite_note-f241-34] Since early 2025, Zelenskyy has called for an unconditional ceasefire and negotiations.[^cite_note-35]

## Early negotiations (February–April 2022)

Russia and Ukraine started peace negotiations in [Belarus](3457.md) on 28 February 2022, four days after the start of the invasion.[^cite_note-36] A second and third round of talks took place on 3 and 7 March 2022[^cite_note-37][^cite_note-DW-2022-03-07-38] in Belarus.[^cite_note-39][^cite_note-40] A fourth and fifth round were held on 10 and 14 March in [Antalya](361527.md), [Turkey](11125639.md).[^cite_note-ADF-41][^cite_note-4thRound-42]

Initially, Russia demanded Ukraine's surrender. However, as Russia's position on the battlefield worsened, its negotiating terms became less demanding.[^cite_note-ForeignAffairs_CharapRadchenko2024-43] The Russians failed in their [attempt to seize Kyiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Kyiv_\(2022\)), taking [heavy casualties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casualties_of_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War) and being forced to retreat.[^cite_note-44][^cite_note-45] After this, the Russians were ready to discuss the status of [Crimea](163045.md).[^cite_note-ForeignAffairs_CharapRadchenko2024-43][^cite_note-\:1-46][^cite_note-\:2-47]

Deputy [Kremlin Chief of Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kremlin_Chief_of_Staff), [Dmitry Kozak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Kozak), said in 2022 that he had negotiated an agreement with Ukraine within a few days of the invasion.[^cite_note-moscowtimes-deal-48] This settlement would have ended hostilities in exchange for guarantees that Ukraine would not join [NATO](21133.md). The agreement was however blocked by Putin, who "expanded his objectives to include annexing swathes of Ukrainian territory".[^cite_note-Reuters14092022-21] A Kremlin spokesman denied the story.[^cite_note-moscowtimes-deal-48] In December 2025 New York Times published a more detailed description of the events of 2022, in which Kozak was categorically against full-scale invasion on Ukraine, realizing the scale of resistance Russian army will face there. He was however sidetracked by Putin for what he perceived as disobedience, when Kozak called representatives of the Ukrainian government on 25 February trying to reach a ceasefire while Putin insisted he will "only accept Ukraine's surrender".[^cite_note-49]

### Belarus and Antalya peace talks

Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) and Belarusian president [Alexander Lukashenko](156589.md) agreed on 27 February that a Ukrainian delegation would meet with Russian officials in Belarus, near the Ukrainian border, without preconditions.[^cite_note-50] It was reported that Lukashenko assured Zelenskyy that all planes, helicopters, and missiles on Belarusian territory would remain on the ground during the negotiations.[^cite_note-51]

On 28 February, talks started in Liaskavičy near the [Belarus-Ukraine border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belarus-Ukraine_border), with Ukrainians including [Davyd Arakhamia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davyd_Arakhamia), [Oleksii Reznikov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleksii_Reznikov) and [Mykhailo Podolyak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mykhailo_Podolyak), and Russians including [Vladimir Medinsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Medinsky).[^cite_note-ForeignAffairs_CharapRadchenko2024-43] Ukrainian president Zelenskyy's office stated that his priorities were an immediate ceasefire and withdrawal of Russian forces from Ukraine.[^cite_note-52] It concluded with no agreement.[^cite_note-53]

On 3 March, the second round of peace talks began in [Kamyanyuki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamyanyuki), Belarus, near the Polish border.[^cite_note-ForeignAffairs_CharapRadchenko2024-43] Both sides agreed to open humanitarian corridors for evacuating civilians.[^cite_note-54] Russia's demands were Ukraine's recognition of Russian-occupied [Crimea](163045.md), independence for separatist-controlled [Luhansk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhansk_People's_Republic) and [Donetsk](42425747.md), and "de-militarisation" and "[de-Nazification](70144390.md#Allegations_of_Nazism)". Ukrainian foreign minister [Dmytro Kuleba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmytro_Kuleba) stated that while his country was ready for talks to resume, Russia's demands had not changed.[^cite_note-abcau220304-18][^cite_note-intellinews220304-55]

Zelenskyy called for Putin to "sit down with me at the negotiating table. But not from 30 meters away, like with [Macron](43671127.md) and [Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz). I don't bite. What are you afraid of?". This was referring to [the long table](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Putin's_meeting_table) Putin uses for meetings.[^cite_note-56] On 5 March, Israeli prime minister [Naftali Bennett](39754840.md) flew to Moscow and held three hours of meetings with Putin, then flew to Germany and held meetings with German chancellor [Olaf Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz). Bennett spoke in advance with Zelenskyy, who had previously asked for his help mediating. He also coordinated with the US, France and Germany.[^cite_note-57] According to *[Al Monitor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Monitor)*, the meetings were instigated by Scholz who made a brief visit to Israel on 3 March and held a long one-on-one meeting with Bennett, which produced the mediation idea.[^cite_note-58]

On 6 March, Ukrainian official [Denys Kireyev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denys_Kireyev) was found dead after being accused of committing 'treason' and working for Russia. Barely a week after attending the first round of peace talks, images of Kireyev's corpse began circulating.[^cite_note-59][^cite_note-60]



[Mykhailo Podolyak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mykhailo_Podolyak), Ukraine's chief negotiator in the early talks

A third round of negotiations began on 7 March in Kamyanyuki, Belarus,[^cite_note-ForeignAffairs_CharapRadchenko2024-43] while Russia continued to attack Ukraine.[^cite_note-61] [Dmitry Peskov](9214683.md) restated Moscow's demands, that Ukraine should agree to change its constitution to enshrine neutrality, accept that Crimea was Russian territory, and recognize Donetsk and Luhansk as independent states; he claimed that Russia was ready to halt military operations "in a moment" if Kyiv agreed to these conditions.[^cite_note-62] Ukrainian negotiator and advisor to the president [Mykhailo Podoliak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mykhailo_Podoliak) tweeted that "there were some small positive shifts regarding logistics of humanitarian corridors."[^cite_note-DW-2022-03-07-38][^cite_note-63]

On 10 March, Russian Foreign Minister [Sergey Lavrov](541346.md) and his Ukrainian counterpart [Dmytro Kuleba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmytro_Kuleba) met for talks in [Antalya](361527.md), [Turkey](11125639.md) with Turkish Foreign Minister [Mevlüt Çavuşoğlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mevl%C3%BCt_%C3%87avu%C5%9Fo%C4%9Flu) as mediator, in the first high-level contact between the two sides since the beginning of the invasion.[^cite_note-ADF-41] Ukraine had attempted to negotiate a 24-hour ceasefire to provide aid and evacuation to civilians, especially in [Mariupol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariupol).[^cite_note-64] After two hours of talks, no agreement was reached.[^cite_note-65] Russian airstrikes on the port city continued.[^cite_note-66]



In the first government delegation to Ukraine since the beginning of the invasion, the prime ministers of Poland, the Czech Republic, and Slovenia met with Zelenskyy in Kyiv on 15 March 2022.

[^cite_note-67]

The fourth round of negotiations began on 14 March by video conference. The talks lasted a few hours and ended without a breakthrough. The two sides resumed talks on 15 March,[^cite_note-4thRound-42] after which Volodymyr Zelenskyy described the talks as beginning to "sound more realistic".[^cite_note-15MarSummary-68]

In mid March, Ukrainian president Zelenskyy said his priorities were to stop the invasion, protect Ukraine's sovereignty, and for Ukraine to be given "real" security guarantees to prevent further Russian aggression.[^cite_note-69]

By 16 March, [Mykhailo Podoliak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mykhailo_Podoliak) was assigned as the chief negotiator for the Ukrainian peace delegation, who indicated that peace negotiations of a 15-point plan would involve the withdrawal of Russian forces from their advanced positions in Ukraine, along with international guarantees for military support in case of renewed Russian aggression, in return for Ukraine ending plans to join NATO.[^cite_note-70]

The two sides again resumed talks by video conference on 16 March.[^cite_note-15MarSummary-68] Later that day, the *[Financial Times](136566.md)* reported that a 15-point plan was being identified by Zelenskyy as more realistic for ending the war.[^cite_note-71][^cite_note-72] After the fourth day of talks on 17 March, Russia said an agreement has not been reached.[^cite_note-73] Following the talks, French Foreign Minister [Jean-Yves Le Drian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Yves_Le_Drian) warned that Russia was only "pretending to negotiate", in line with a strategy it has used elsewhere.[^cite_note-RFI-2022-03-17-74]

On 20 March, Turkey's Foreign Minister [Mevlüt Çavuşoğlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mevl%C3%BCt_%C3%87avu%C5%9Fo%C4%9Flu) described the negotiations as "making progress". He had earlier visited Ukraine and Russia.[^cite_note-75]

Following his address to the Israeli parliament, Zelenskyy thanked [Naftali Bennett](39754840.md) for his efforts in encouraging peace talks, and suggested that they might take place in Jerusalem.[^cite_note-76]

The fifth round of talks, by video conference on 21 March, failed to achieve a breakthrough. Zelenskyy called for direct talks with Putin to end the war.[^cite_note-77] Sergey Lavrov said direct talks between the two presidents would only go ahead once both sides are closer to reaching a settlement.[^cite_note-78]

### Istanbul peace talks

Further peace talks between Russia and Ukraine began in Istanbul on 29 March 2022.[^cite_note-guardian220328-79]

Before the talks, in an interview with Russian journalists on 27 March, Zelenskyy said he was willing to discuss Ukraine becoming a [neutral country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neutral_country), in exchange for security guarantees, and that he was willing to negotiate the status of Russian-occupied Crimea and the Donbas. He said there could be no peace deal without a ceasefire and Russian troop withdrawal. Russia's media watchdog [Roskomnadzor](15773661.md) ordered Russian media not to publish the interview.[^cite_note-80]

Ahead of the 29 March meeting, Turkish president [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) said Ukraine was willing to agree to 4 out of Russia's 6 demands.[^cite_note-Erdoğan7406819-81] He said Ukraine was willing to give up plans for [NATO membership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine%E2%80%93NATO_relations) and willing to make Russian Ukraine's second official language, but would not recognize Russian occupation of any part of Ukraine.[^cite_note-Erdoğan7406819-81] Estonian prime minister, [Kaja Kallas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaja_Kallas), agreed with French minister Le Drian that any Russian offers should be regarded with skepticism, based on a history of Russian unreliability in peace negotiations.[^cite_note-82][^cite_note-RFI-2022-03-17-74] Russia's Ministry of Defense announced "drastic reduction of military activity" on the [Northern front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_front_of_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-83][^cite_note-cnn2903-84]

#### Istanbul Communiqué



[Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Wall_of_Remembrance_of_the_Fallen_for_Ukraine) to Ukrainian soldiers killed in the Russo-Ukrainian War in 2022

The negotiating teams produced the Istanbul Communiqué, "Key Provisions of the Treaty on Ukraine's Security Guarantees"—a framework of a possible treaty.

It proposed that Ukraine would end NATO membership plans and become a [neutral country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neutral_country), in exchange for security guarantees; whereby Russia and various Western countries would be obliged to help Ukraine militarily if it were attacked. It also proposed limits on [Ukraine's military](42799055.md). Ukraine would be allowed to apply for EU membership, and there would be a 15-year consultation period on the status of [Crimea](163045.md).[^cite_note-ForeignAffairs_CharapRadchenko2024-43][^cite_note-\:1-46][^cite_note-\:2-47][^cite_note-reuters220329-30]

#### Draft treaties

The discovery of the [Bucha massacre](70455343.md) at the beginning of April 2022 sparked outrage in Ukraine and internationally, but work on drafting a treaty continued.

One disagreement was over security guarantees. The Istanbul Communiqué said that if Ukraine was attacked and asked for help, the guarantor states would be obliged to help Ukraine militarily. The Russians then demanded this be changed so that the guarantor states would only defend Ukraine from an attack if "all guarantor states" agreed, including Russia. This meant that the Kremlin could [veto](74661.md) a military intervention to defend Ukraine from an attack, including attacks by Russian forces or proxy forces. The Ukrainians rejected the demand.[^cite_note-ForeignAffairs_CharapRadchenko2024-43][^cite_note-\:1-46][^cite_note-\:2-47]

Another disagreement was over the size of Ukraine's military. The Ukrainians wanted a peacetime army of 250,000; the Russians demanded a maximum of 85,000, which is much smaller than the standing army Ukraine had before the invasion.[^cite_note-ForeignAffairs_CharapRadchenko2024-43] The Russians also demanded severe limits on the amount and kinds of weaponry Ukraine could have, without any limits on Russia's military.[^cite_note-ForeignAffairs_CharapRadchenko2024-43]

A third disagreement was the Russians' new demands for Ukraine to revoke several of its domestic laws, dealing with [contentious aspects of Soviet-era history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decolonization_in_Ukraine), such as the role of Ukrainian nationalists in World War II. The Ukrainians refused to allow their policies on historical memory to be determined by Russia.[^cite_note-ForeignAffairs_CharapRadchenko2024-43]

The negotiations avoided dealing with borders and territory. This was to be discussed by Putin and Zelenskyy at a planned summit. The likely outcome, according to *Foreign Affairs*, was that Putin would demand to hold all the territory his army had occupied, and Zelenskyy would have refused.[^cite_note-ForeignAffairs_CharapRadchenko2024-43]

On 7 April, Russian foreign minister [Sergey Lavrov](541346.md) said that Ukraine's latest draft treaty was "unacceptable" and diverged from the terms negotiators had agreed on. Ukrainian negotiator [Mykhailo Podolyak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mykhailo_Podolyak) said Lavrov's comments were a tactic to draw attention away from [Russian war crime accusations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_crimes_in_the_2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine). Lavrov added, "Despite all the provocations, the Russian delegation will continue with the negotiation process".[^cite_note-85]

In *[Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Affairs)*, political scientists Samuel Charap and [Sergey Radchenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergey_Radchenko) studied several drafts of the treaty, interviewed participants in the talks and officials in several Western governments, and reviewed public statements by and interviews with Ukrainian and Russian officials. They argued that the evidence showed "mutual willingness" by Zelenskyy and Putin to "consider far-reaching concessions to end the war". They said an agreement providing Ukraine with multilateral security guarantees, permanent neutrality and EU membership in the long term, was "almost finalised", but key issues were not resolved.[^cite_note-ForeignAffairs_CharapRadchenko2024-43] Another article in *Foreign Affairs* by [Fiona Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiona_Hill_\(presidential_advisor\)) and [Angela Stent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angela_Stent) said that negotiators also "appeared to have tentatively agreed" that Russia would withdraw to its pre-invasion positions.[^cite_note-foreignaff220825-86] The *[New York Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Times)* also later published several drafts of the treaty, and gave its analysis based on speaking with more than twelve Ukrainian, Russian and Western participants and other people close to the negotiations.[^cite_note-NYT_UA_RU_peace_elusive-87][^cite_note-NYT_sticking_points-88]

On 17 June 2023, Putin showed African leaders what he says was one of the draft treaties.[^cite_note-peace-initiative-7142034-89] Putin claimed the document had been signed by the head of the Ukrainian delegation and that there were 18 articles.[^cite_note-Arakhamia7430282-90] In November 2023, Ukrainian negotiator [Davyd Arakhamia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davyd_Arakhamia) refuted Putin's claim that Ukraine had signed any agreement in Turkey, because the delegation did not even have the legal right to sign one.[^cite_note-Arakhamia7430282-90]

### End of negotiations



Some of the victims of the [Bucha massacre](70455343.md), uncovered in early April 2022

Charap and Radchenko argue that the negotiations failed because of several things: the failure to focus on reaching a [ceasefire](592591.md) first of all; disagreement between Russia and Ukraine over key issues (outlined [above](#Draft_treaties)) including Ukraine's military and security; the unwillingness of Ukraine's Western partners to give absolute security guarantees; Russian [atrocities against civilians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_crimes_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine); and Ukraine's heightened confidence in a military solution after Russia's retreat from the north. The negotiations also did not deal with the return of Russian-occupied territory.[^cite_note-ForeignAffairs_CharapRadchenko2024-43] Some saw the negotiations only as a distraction by Russia to allow regrouping.[^cite_note-ForeignAffairs_CharapRadchenko2024-43]

Polish diplomat [Jakub Kumoch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jakub_Kumoch) criticized the analysis of Charap and Radchenko, arguing that they "accepted the Russian narrative". He said the lack of consensus over borders, disarmament and security guarantees, blocked any chance of agreement.[^cite_note-91]

Putin claimed that Ukraine rejected an agreement because of pressure from the West.[^cite_note-Repeat-92] According to a story in *[Ukrainska Pravda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainska_Pravda)*, British prime minister [Boris Johnson](19065069.md) arrived in Kyiv on 9 April with two messages: the UK would not sign up to security guarantees with Putin, and Putin should be pressured rather than negotiated with.[^cite_note-pravdaua220505-93][^cite_note-Repeat-92] Three days after Johnson left Kyiv, Putin said that talks with Ukraine "had turned into a dead end".[^cite_note-pravdaua220505-93] Israeli prime minister Naftali Bennett said that Ukraine's Western allies had stopped an agreement.[^cite_note-94] Later he expressed doubts regarding the desirability of such a deal.[^cite_note-95][^cite_note-96]

Ukrainian lead negotiator [Davyd Arakhamia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davyd_Arakhamia) later said that Ukraine's neutrality was the key Russian demand during the negotiations and that the Western countries advised Ukraine not to rely on security guarantees.[^cite_note-97][^cite_note-98] Both Zelenskyy and Arakhamia denied that Western countries stopped Kyiv from signing an agreement.[^cite_note-Arakhamia7430282-90][^cite_note-99] He said that Russia had not offered any security guarantees and the Ukrainian delegation did not trust Russia to uphold an agreement.[^cite_note-Arakhamia7430282-90][^cite_note-100][^cite_note-101]

On 16 April 2022, Zelenskyy tried to break the deadlock by proposing two separate treaties: an agreement on security guarantees "from those who are prepared to give such security guarantees", and a bilateral agreement between Ukraine and Russia. Several countries had shown willingness to become guarantors of Ukraine's security, but none had committed. Zelenskyy said "Moscow would like to have one treaty that would resolve all the issues. However, not everyone sees themselves at the table with Russia".[^cite_note-102] The Russians had demanded they be included as one of the guarantors and be given a [veto](74661.md) on military intervention to defend Ukraine.[^cite_note-ForeignAffairs_CharapRadchenko2024-43]

## April 2022–December 2024

On 11 April, the chancellor of Austria, [Karl Nehammer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karl_Nehammer), visited Moscow and had "very direct, open and hard" talks with Putin. Afterwards, Nehammer said he was not optimistic about a peaceful resolution, adding that Russia's offensive "is being prepared with determination".[^cite_note-103] On 26 April, the secretary-general of the United Nations, [António Guterres](471806.md), visited Russia to meet Putin and Lavrov. Afterwards, he told reporters it was "clear that there are two different positions on what is happening in Ukraine," and suggested that Putin and Lavrov had given him little hope of stopping the invasion.[^cite_note-104]

On 13 May 2022, US secretary of defense [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin) initiated a telephone conversation with Russian minister of defense [Sergei Shoigu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergei_Shoigu), the first call since 18 February, before the invasion. The call lasted about an hour with Austin urging an immediate ceasefire.[^cite_note-thehill-20220513-105][^cite_note-dod-20220513-106] On 15 May, Putin convened the [Collective Security Treaty Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collective_Security_Treaty_Organization), consisting of Russia, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Armenia, Tajikistan, and Belarus, to discuss issues of peace and border security related to Ukraine and NATO.[^cite_note-107]

President Zelenskyy denounced suggestions by former US diplomat [Henry Kissinger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Kissinger) that Ukraine should [cede control of Crimea and Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_national_border_changes_\(1914%E2%80%93present\)) to Russia in exchange for peace.[^cite_note-108] On 25 May, Zelenskyy said that Ukraine would not agree to peace until Russia agreed to return Crimea and the Donbas region to Ukraine.[^cite_note-109] Zelenskyy stressed that "Ukrainians are not ready to give away their land, to accept that these territories belong to Russia." He emphasized that Ukrainians own the land of Ukraine.[^cite_note-110] As of September 2022. Kissinger changed his initial suggestion and presented it at the World Economic Forum in Davos in January 2023, where he announced that NATO membership for Ukraine would be an "appropriate outcome" from his perspective.[^cite_note-111]

### Ukrainian offensives and Russian annexations (August 2022–January 2023)

Further information: [Russian annexation of Donetsk, Kherson, Luhansk and Zaporizhzhia oblasts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts)



The four Ukrainian provinces that Russia [annexed in September 2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts), despite them not being under full Russian control. Putin demanded Ukraine recognize Russian sovereignty over these provinces as a condition for peace talks.

[^cite_note-112]

Ukraine began [a counteroffensive in Kherson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Kherson_counteroffensive) on 29 August 2022[^cite_note-113] that culminated in the [Liberation of Kherson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_of_Kherson) that November,[^cite_note-114] alongside a [successful counteroffensive in Kharkiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Kharkiv_counteroffensive) that began in September.[^cite_note-115] In September, Ukraine rejected a peace plan proposed by [Mexico](3966054.md),[^cite_note-116] and Zelenskyy addressed the [UN General Assembly](31957.md), laying out five "non-negotiable" peace conditions: "just punishment" of Russia, protection of life by "all available means allowed under the [UN charter](88805.md)", restoring security and territorial integrity, foreign security guarantees for Ukraine, and determination for Ukraine to continue in self-defense.[^cite_note-117][^cite_note-118] Zelenskyy said he saw little chance for peace talks unless Russia withdrew from Ukraine.[^cite_note-119] On 30 September, Russia announced the [annexation of four Ukrainian oblasts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts)—[Donetsk](494889.md), [Luhansk](494904.md), [Zaporizhzhia](494936.md), and [Kherson](1273370.md)—though it did not fully control any of them.[^cite_note-\:11-120] In response, Ukraine applied for NATO membership and Zelenskyy announced that Ukraine would not hold peace talks with Russia while Putin was president,[^cite_note-\:11-120][^cite_note-reuters220930-16] signing a presidential decree declaring negotiations with Putin "impossible".[^cite_note-an1-121][^cite_note-wapo1-122]

After [October 2022 missile strikes on Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_2022_missile_strikes_on_Ukraine), China and India expressed concern and called for de-escalation and dialogue.[^cite_note-123] Erdoğan said that Putin "is now more open to possible peace talks" and Ukraine "was not rejecting such peace talks";[^cite_note-124] Russia said it was ready for renewed dialogue,[^cite_note-125] but Ukraine said it did not believe Russia was truly committed to peace and that it sought only a break to rebuild its military capabilities for future attacks.[^cite_note-meduza221026-126] In October, [Elon Musk](909036.md) suggested a peace settlement in which Ukraine would permanently cede Crimea to Russia and assure its continued water supply, drop its bid to join NATO, and submit to UN-supervised referendums on the future of the Russian-annexed territories.[^cite_note-127] Russia welcomed the proposal, though Zelenskyy rebuffed it as "pro-Russia".[^cite_note-128][^cite_note-129][^cite_note-130]



US General [Mark Milley](38091005.md) in May 2022. Milley was among the first prominent Western officials to publicly urge a negotiated settlement to the conflict.

[^cite_note-131]

On 7 November, Zelenskyy listed new conditions for negotiating with Russia: "One more time: restoration of territorial integrity, respect for the U.N. charter, compensation for all material losses caused by the war, \[and] punishment for every war criminal and guarantees that this does not happen again."[^cite_note-132][^cite_note-133] [Mark Milley](38091005.md), the US [Joint Chiefs of Staff Chairman](364458.md), said Ukraine should find a "political solution" with Russia because the war was unwinnable for it by military means;[^cite_note-134][^cite_note-135][^cite_note-136] a later *[New York Times](30680.md)* report said that US secretary of state [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) insisted that Ukraine continue fighting in response to Milley's statement.[^cite_note-137] Ukraine presented the detailed [10-point peace proposal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine's_Peace_Formula) later in November at a [G20](2206608.md) meeting.[^cite_note-AJE_Zelenskyy_10pt_plan-138] In December, Ukrainian foreign minister [Dmytro Kuleba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmytro_Kuleba) suggested a February 2023 peace summit at the UN mediated by secretary-general [António Guterres](471806.md) to which Russia would only be invited if it faced an international court for war crimes.[^cite_note-ap221226-139] Later that month, Russian officials said it would only resume peace talks with Ukraine if it recognized Russian sovereignty over the annexed regions.[^cite_note-voa221230-22][^cite_note-140] Peskov said in January 2023 that "there is currently no prospect for diplomatic means of settling the situation around Ukraine."[^cite_note-141]

### Continuing war and international proposals (February–September 2023)

Further information: [China's Position on the Political Settlement of the Ukrainian Crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China's_Position_on_the_Political_Settlement_of_the_Ukrainian_Crisis)



Brazilian president [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](99328.md), who proposed a peace plan involving the [status of Crimea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_status_of_Crimea).

[^cite_note-rferl-brazil-142]

In February 2023, at the [59th Munich Security Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/59th_Munich_Security_Conference), Chinese foreign policy chief [Wang Yi](18211536.md) said Chinese leader [Xi Jinping](2017814.md) would present a peace proposal for the war in Ukraine.[^cite_note-143][^cite_note-144] On 24 February, China's government published a 12-point paper of "[China's Position on the Political Settlement of the Ukraine Crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China's_Position_on_the_Political_Settlement_of_the_Ukrainian_Crisis)", receiving mixed responses from Western leaders.[^cite_note-145][^cite_note-146][^cite_note-147] At the [UN General Assembly](31957.md), China's deputy ambassador to the UN proposed a plan involving a ceasefire, dialogue, security guarantees for Russia, protection of civilians, and the upholding of territorial integrity; China also said that the West had exacerbated the conflict by supplying Ukraine with weaponry.[^cite_note-148][^cite_note-149] Zelenskyy said that "I plan to meet Xi Jinping and believe this will be beneficial for our countries and for security in the world", though a time or place for a meeting was not set.[^cite_note-150] The spokeswoman of Russia's foreign ministry, [Maria Zakharova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Zakharova), said Russia appreciated China's efforts to resolve the conflict and was "open to achieving the goals of the [special military operation](70156528.md) by political and diplomatic means" but that it would entail recognising the "new territorial realities" of Russia's annexations.[^cite_note-151] Peskov said that for now, we don't see any of the conditions that are needed to bring this whole story towards peace."[^cite_note-chinese-peskov-152]



Chinese leader [Xi Jinping](2017814.md) with Putin during Xi's [2023 visit to Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_visit_by_Xi_Jinping_to_Russia)

Chinese foreign minister [Qin Gang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qin_Gang) said sanctions and political pressure would not resolve the conflict, which he said "seems to be driven by an invisible hand pushing for the protraction and escalation", and that "the process of peace talks should begin as soon as possible".[^cite_note-153] Xi said that China's peace proposal reflected world opinion ahead of [his visit to Russia on 20–22 March 2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_visit_by_Xi_Jinping_to_Russia).[^cite_note-154] Blinken responded by saying "the world should not be fooled by any tactical move by Russia, supported by China or any other country, to freeze the war on its own terms."[^cite_note-guardian230321-155] On 26 April 2023, over a month after Xi's summit with Putin, Xi Jinping held a phone call with Zelenskyy.[^cite_note-156] The Chinese readout of the Xi–Zelenskyy call included little concrete information about the start of a peace process;[^cite_note-157] in May, European officials criticized China's peace plan as an attempt at "freezing" the conflict in place and splitting the West in pushing Ukraine to a ceasefire.[^cite_note-wsj230526-158] Earlier, Peskov had said that Russia has "to achieve our goals" and that "Right now this is only possible by military means."[^cite_note-159]

In April 2023, some Ukrainian officials said they would be willing to discuss the status of Crimea.[^cite_note-160][^cite_note-foreignpol230411-161] That month, Brazilian president [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](99328.md) suggested that Ukraine should "give up Crimea" in exchange for Russian withdrawal from all territory it has occupied since February 2022.[^cite_note-\:15-162] Lula condemned Russia's violation of Ukraine's territorial integrity but said that Zelenskyy "cannot want everything";[^cite_note-\:15-162][^cite_note-163] he said Brazil advocates a "negotiated political solution to the conflict" and expressed concern over "global consequences" of the war "in terms of [food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022%E2%80%932023_global_food_crises) and energy security".[^cite_note-aljazeera-brazil-164] Brazilian foreign policy adviser [Celso Amorim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celso_Amorim) said "the ideal peace for the Ukrainians and the Russians" required "concessions".[^cite_note-aljazeera-brazil-164] Ukraine rejected Lula's proposal.[^cite_note-165] Lula said after visiting China that "the United States needs to stop encouraging war and start talking about peace", to which US national security spokesman [John Kirby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kirby_\(admiral\)) responded by accusing Lula of "parroting Russian and Chinese propaganda" and suggesting that the US and Europe were responsible for Russia's invasion.[^cite_note-166]



Chinese peace envoy [Li Hui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Li_Hui_\(diplomat\)) meets with Ukrainian foreign minister [Dmytro Kuleba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmytro_Kuleba) in Kyiv, May 2023.

On 7 April, Russian foreign minister [Sergey Lavrov](541346.md) said Russia rejects the United States-led "[unipolar world order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polarity_\(international_relations\))" and that peace talks with Ukraine should focus on creating a "new world order".[^cite_note-moscow230507-23] Lula proposed creating a "peace club" of a group of [developing countries](78449.md), including Brazil and China, that would negotiate peace in Ukraine.[^cite_note-rferl-brazil-142] On 30 April, [Pope Francis](1687680.md) said the [Holy See](13393.md) was taking part in a secret "peace mission" to end the war.[^cite_note-167] In May 2023, UN secretary-general [António Guterres](471806.md) said peace negotiations to end the war were "not possible at this moment" because Russia was "completely absorbed in this war" and "convinced that they can win".[^cite_note-168] On 16 May, South African president [Cyril Ramaphosa](1089194.md) said that a group of African leaders had created a new initiative for peace in Ukraine.[^cite_note-169] Lula said that the countries of the [Global South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_North_and_Global_South), including Brazil, India, Indonesia, and China, "want peace", but both Putin and Zelenskyy "are convinced that they are going to win the war" and are refusing talks.[^cite_note-170] On 22 May, Danish foreign minister [Lars Løkke Rasmussen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lars_L%C3%B8kke_Rasmussen) said Denmark was willing to host a summit in July 2023 aimed at "finding peace between Ukraine and Russia", and said that "it is necessary to build interest and involvement from countries like India, Brazil and China."[^cite_note-171]

#### June–September 2023

On 3 June, [Indonesia](14579.md)'s defense minister [Prabowo Subianto](2829084.md) proposed a multi-point peace plan at the [Shangri-La Dialogue](5410193.md) security summit in Singapore including a ceasefire and establishing a demilitarised zone observed and monitored by the [United Nations Peacekeeping Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_Nations_peacekeeping_missions).[^cite_note-172] He said that a UN referendum should be held "to ascertain objectively the wishes of the majority of the inhabitants of the various disputed areas".[^cite_note-173] Prabowo's proposal was criticised by EU foreign policy chief [Josep Borrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep_Borrell).[^cite_note-174]

Ukrainian defense minister [Oleksii Reznikov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleksii_Reznikov) said that the peace plans presented by China, Brazil and Indonesia are attempts at mediation on behalf of Russia, saying that "they all currently want to be mediators on Russia's side. That's why this sort of mediation currently doesn't fit for us at all because they aren't impartial."[^cite_note-175] Reznikov said that Ukraine is willing to accept China as a mediator for peace talks between Russia and Ukraine only if Beijing could convince Russia to withdraw from all the territories it has [occupied in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian-occupied_territories_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-176]



South African president [Cyril Ramaphosa](1089194.md) promoted the African peace plan but Putin rejected a complete withdrawal of Russian forces from Ukraine, and Zelenskyy rejected a ceasefire without Russian withdrawal from Ukraine.

In June, a delegation from Africa, including representatives from [South Africa](17416221.md), [Egypt](8087628.md), [Senegal](27278.md), [Congo-Brazzaville](19599929.md), [Comoros](5403.md), [Zambia](34415.md), and [Uganda](31816.md), visited Ukraine and Russia to call for peace.[^cite_note-177] Both Russia and Ukraine welcomed the African leaders' mission, but Ukrainian Foreign Minister [Dmytro Kuleba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmytro_Kuleba) warned that "Any peace initiative should respect the territorial integrity of Ukraine, it should not imply, even in-between the lines, any cessation of Ukrainian territory to Russia. Second, any peace plan should not lead to the freezing of the conflict."[^cite_note-178] After a meeting with African leaders in Kyiv, Ukrainian president Volodymyr Zelenskyy said that peace talks with Russia would be possible only after Moscow withdraws its forces from the entire occupied territory.[^cite_note-179] The African delegation was in Kyiv during [a Russian missile strike on the city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022%E2%80%932023_Russian_strikes_against_Ukrainian_infrastructure).[^cite_note-180]

On 23 July 2024, Ukrainian foreign minister [Dmytro Kuleba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmytro_Kuleba) visited China for talks with Chinese Foreign Minister [Wang Yi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Yi_\(politician\)) on ways to achieve a peaceful end to the war with Russia. This was the first such bilateral visit since 2012.[^cite_note-reuters-20240723-181][^cite_note-ukrn-20240724-182]

On 17 June, South African president [Cyril Ramaphosa](1089194.md) and other African leaders met Russian president Vladimir Putin in St. Petersburg. Ramaphosa told Putin that the war must end.[^cite_note-183] Putin rejected the delegation's peace plan based on accepting Ukraine's internationally recognized [borders](31351346.md).[^cite_note-184] Putin also claimed that it was "virtually impossible to implement a ceasefire", adding that "Ukraine is advancing, they're on a strategic offensive, how do we hold our fire when they're advancing on us?".[^cite_note-185]

The Ukrainian Peace Plan has been publicized at a series of meetings that began on 25 June 2023.[^cite_note-cnn1-186][^cite_note-rtrs1-187] The first conference, which was held in Copenhagen, was attended by delegates from 15 countries.[^cite_note-cafe1-188]

On 29 July, following a meeting with African leaders at the [2023 Russia–Africa Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Russia%E2%80%93Africa_Summit) in St. Petersburg, Russian president Vladimir Putin rejected a ceasefire and peace talks with Ukraine due to the ongoing [Ukrainian counter-offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Ukrainian_counteroffensive) against Russian forces in [Russia-occupied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian-occupied_territories_of_Ukraine) southeastern Ukraine, saying "We cannot cease fire when we are under attack."[^cite_note-189]



Indonesian defense minister [Prabowo Subianto](2829084.md) proposed a new peace initiative at the [Shangri-La Dialogue](5410193.md) security summit in Singapore.

On 5 and 6 August 2023 [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) hosted multinational peace talks attended by 40 countries (including China) in [Jeddah](277718.md) focusing on Ukrainian president Volodymyr Zelenskyy's proposed peace plan.[^cite_note-SAsummit286936-190][^cite_note-191] Russia did not participate in the talks.[^cite_note-SAsummit286936-190] Prior to the summit [Andriy Yermak](63086056.md), Zelenskyy's chief of staff, said there would be discussions on the Ukrainian Peace Formula, which "contains 10 fundamental points, the implementation of which will not only ensure peace for Ukraine, but also create mechanisms to counter future conflicts in the world".[^cite_note-192] After the summit no joint statement was issued.[^cite_note-SAsummit286936-190] The participants did agree to create working groups to develop details of key themes prominent in Zelenskyy's proposed peace plan (that calls for Russian troops to withdraw from all of Ukraine's territory as part of a peace deal) while a parallel ambassadors group would continue technical work on the issues.[^cite_note-SAsummit286936-190]

On 27 September 2023, Russian Foreign Minister [Sergey Lavrov](541346.md) said in an interview with [TASS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TASS) that Russia was "ready to make agreements" on Ukraine if they would "take the situation on the ground" and Russian security interests into account.[^cite_note-Lav1681505-193] According to Lavrov one of the Russian security interest was "the need to prevent the creation of a hostile Nazi regime near Russian borders."[^cite_note-Lav1681505-193] Lavrov also said that Russia did not see "any serious proposals from the West" and he argued that they were promoting [Ukrainian president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_president) Volodymyr Zelenskyy ['peace formula'](#September_2022) as "the sole basis for negotiations" and that there was "no point in discussing it as it is nothing but an ultimatum."[^cite_note-Lav1681505-193] Lavrov also praised the [June 2023 African](#June_2023), [Arab League](52625.md) (although formally the Arab League never submitted its own peace proposal[^cite_note-SAsummit286936-190]), [Chinese](#Chinese_peace_proposal_\(2023\)), [Brazilian](#April_2023) and peace proposals "from other countries" since he claimed "All of them were guided by a sincere desire to facilitate an agreement, which would consider the root causes of the current situation and the need to eliminate them, and will also ensure equal security for all parties."[^cite_note-Lav1681505-193]

[Slovakia](26830.md)'s Prime Minister [Robert Fico](4127226.md) said that sending more and more [weapons to Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_aid_to_Ukraine_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War) has only encouraged more killings and endless war, and instead efforts should be made to reach a cease-fire and push Russia and Ukraine toward peace talks. In September 2023, he stated in an interview: "Why don't we force the warring parties, use the weight of the EU and the US to make them sit down and find some sort of compromise that would guarantee security for Ukraine?"[^cite_note-194][^cite_note-195] He praised the peace plans to end the war put forward by the [Vatican](13393.md), Brazil and China.[^cite_note-196] Fico said his government would do everything possible to start peace talks between Russia and Ukraine.[^cite_note-197]

### Putin's peace terms and ceasefire proposals (October 2023–June 2024)

Further information: [Tucker Carlson's interview with Vladimir Putin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tucker_Carlson's_interview_with_Vladimir_Putin)

In October 2023, the [Ukraine Defense Contact Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Defense_Contact_Group) began confidential talks with Ukrainian officials over the broad outline of possible peace talks.[^cite_note-nbc-20231103-198] Later that month, delegates from 65 countries met in Malta to discuss Ukraine's peace plan.[^cite_note-bloom1-199][^cite_note-en2-200][^cite_note-cnbc1-201] In November, Zelenskyy rejected peace talks; he said he is "not ready to speak with the terrorists, because their word is nothing", and that "the world can switch to diplomacy" only if Russia withdraws from Ukraine.[^cite_note-f241-34] French president [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) said Russia was committing "imperialism and colonialism" in Ukraine and that France and other countries had the "duty" to help Ukraine's defense, but said that maybe the time would come eventually for fair peace negotiations and a solution with Russia.[^cite_note-202] Putin said Russia was always "ready for talks" to end the "tragedy" of the war and blamed Ukraine for rejecting peace with Russia.[^cite_note-203] The next month, representatives of 83 states and three international organizations met in Kyiv to discuss the implementation of Ukraine's ten-point peace plan.[^cite_note-204] On 15 December, Putin said in [his annual press conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct_Line_with_Vladimir_Putin) that "there will only be peace in Ukraine when we achieve our aims" of "denazification, demilitarisation and \[Ukraine's] neutral status".[^cite_note-utinTalksDL2367711802P-205] He said that Ukraine did not give the now-annexed regions "the attention they were due" and that he would work to integrate them into Russia's "economic and social life".[^cite_note-DLVP23Meduza-206]



Zelenskyy meeting US secretary of state [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) at the [World Economic Forum](172185.md), January 2024

*The New York Times* reported in December 2023 that Putin had been signaling through intermediaries since at least September 2022 that "he is open to a ceasefire that freezes the fighting along the current lines";[^cite_note-207][^cite_note-NYTimes-2023-12-23-208] a former senior Russian official told the *Times* that Putin "is willing to stop at the current positions" but is "not willing to retreat one meter."[^cite_note-209] In January 2024, US president [Joe Biden](145422.md) and his national security advisor [Jake Sullivan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jake_Sullivan) privately rejected a proposal from Putin for a ceasefire in the war; Sullivan told Putin's foreign policy adviser [Yuri Ushakov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuri_Ushakov) that the United States would only discuss a ceasefire with Ukraine's participation.[^cite_note-reuters-ceasefire-210][^cite_note-Ukrainska_Pravda-211] Putin's proposal reportedly involved a freeze along the current front lines that allowed Russia to keep occupied territory;[^cite_note-Ukrainska_Pravda-211][^cite_note-reuters-ceasefire-210] Russia denied that Putin had secretly proposed a ceasefire.[^cite_note-212] Zelenskyy said he does not feel pressure from allies to agree to a ceasefire[^cite_note-213] and said "any frozen conflict \[with Russia] will eventually reignite".[^cite_note-214] Another meeting to discuss Ukraine's peace formula was held in [Davos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davos) at the [World Economic Forum](172185.md), with delegates from 83 nations.[^cite_note-en1-215][^cite_note-kp1-216]



Putin with American journalist [Tucker Carlson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tucker_Carlson) in [a 2024 interview](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tucker_Carlson's_interview_with_Vladimir_Putin)

Putin said that he was ready to negotiate with Zelenskyy during [a February 2024 interview with Tucker Carlson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tucker_Carlson's_interview_with_Vladimir_Putin).[^cite_note-217] Putin did not name any conditions for peace negotiations[^cite_note-218] and avoided a question of whether he would "be satisfied" with the territory Russia currently held.[^cite_note-219] He said that Russia had not yet achieved its war aims because "one of them is denazification", which involved "the prohibition of all kinds of [neo-Nazi](54361.md) movements."[^cite_note-220] On 4 March, former Russian president and deputy [Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_Council_of_Russia) chairman [Dmitry Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Medvedev) ruled out peace talks with Ukraine's current leadership and said any future Ukrainian government would need to accept Russian occupation, while describing Ukraine as part of Russia.[^cite_note-221][^cite_note-222] [Pope Francis](1687680.md) said that Ukraine should have talks with Russia "before things get worse", saying that it should "have the courage to negotiate" and that doing so is not equivalent to surrendering.[^cite_note-223] The next week, Putin said it would be "ridiculous" to "negotiate just because \[Ukraine is] running out of ammunition", but that he was "ready for a serious conversation" to end the war.[^cite_note-aa-224]

In May 2024, Russian sources told [Reuters](18998750.md) that Putin was ready to halt the war along the current front lines, saying that Putin believed that the West was trying to derail ceasefire talks.[^cite_note-225][^cite_note-226] Italian defense minister [Guido Crosetto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guido_Crosetto) said Ukraine must "leave open the possibility of negotiating an immediate truce and initiating peace talks in the coming months."[^cite_note-227] On 24 May, China and Brazil jointly presented a new six-point peace plan that called for an international peace conference "held at a proper time that is recognised by both Russia and Ukraine, with equal participation of all parties as well as fair discussion of all peace plans."[^cite_note-228] On 14 June, Putin outlined Russian terms for a ceasefire, including the complete handover of all annexed provinces, including the parts not controlled by Russia;[^cite_note-Putin7460781-229] the requirement of Ukraine to become a neutral country, abandon plans to join NATO, and never seek to [develop nuclear weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_and_weapons_of_mass_destruction); respect for the rights of [Russian-speakers in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language_in_Ukraine); the lifting of [sanctions against Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_sanctions_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War);[^cite_note-Putin7460781-229] and the "demilitarization and denazification" of Ukraine.[^cite_note-Putin1803657-14] Putin said his proposal was "not about freezing the conflict but about its final resolution", and said that if turned down by Ukraine and its allies, then it would be "their political and moral responsibility for continuing the bloodshed".[^cite_note-Putin7460781-229] In response, Zelenskyy said that Putin's messages were "ultimatum messages", calling them "the same thing [Hitler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hitler) did, when he said '[give me a part of Czechoslovakia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Agreement) and it'll end here'."[^cite_note-Putin7460781-229]

### Ukraine peace summit and Zelenskyy's victory plan (June–October 2024)

Further information: [June 2024 Ukraine peace summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_2024_Ukraine_peace_summit), [Victory Plan for Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victory_Plan_for_Ukraine), and [2024 visits by Viktor Orbán to Russia and China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_visits_by_Viktor_Orb%C3%A1n_to_Russia_and_China)



The [2024 Ukraine Peace Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_2024_Ukraine_peace_summit)

On 15–16 June 2024, representatives from 92 nations and 8 international organizations attended an international [summit on peace in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_2024_Ukraine_peace_summit)[^cite_note-SwissFDFA_summit_on_peace_20240524-230][^cite_note-SwissFDFA_3topics_selected-231][^cite_note-FDFA_participating_countries-232] in [Nidwalden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nidwalden), Switzerland;[^cite_note-VBS_CH_host_highlevel-233] Russia was not invited.[^cite_note-SwissFDFA_summit_on_peace_20240524-230][^cite_note-FDFA_participating_countries-232] The final statement from the summit, [Joint Communiqué on a Peace Framework](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Summit%20on%20Peace%20in%20Ukraine:%20Joint%20Communiqu%C3%A9%20on%20a%20Peace%20Framework), was backed by a majority of participants. It said that "Ukrainian nuclear power plants and installations, including [Zaporizhzhia Nuclear Power Plant](1763005.md), must [operate safely and securely](71700643.md) under full sovereign control of Ukraine and in line with [IAEA](14984.md) principles and under its supervision". It also declared that "Any [threat or use of nuclear weapons in the context of the ongoing war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_risk_during_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) against Ukraine is inadmissible"; that "Attacks on merchant ships in ports and along the entire route" and "against civilian ports and civilian port infrastructure, are unacceptable"; and that "Ukrainian agricultural products should be securely and freely provided to interested third countries." It said that ""all [prisoners of war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prisoners_of_war_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) must be released" and that all children and "other [Ukrainian civilians who were unlawfully detained](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_filtration_camps_for_Ukrainians), must be returned to Ukraine".[^cite_note-FDFA_joint_communique-234] Some delegates did not endorse the communiqué;[^cite_note-235][^cite_note-FDFA_joint_communique_17June_version2-236] as of 2024, 89 states and six organisations had signed it.[^cite_note-FDFA_joint_communique_26September_version1-237] Later in June, retired Lieutenant General [Keith Kellogg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keith_Kellogg) and [Frederick H. Fleitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frederick_H._Fleitz) presented former US president and then-presidential candidate [Donald Trump](4848272.md) a detailed plan to end the war in Ukraine. The plan proposes a ceasefire along the current front lines, with the US continuing arms supplies to Ukraine if it agreed to a ceasefire and peace talks, and increasing supplies if Russia refused. Ukraine would not have to formally cede territory, but Russia would retain *[de facto](43594.md)* control of occupied territories and Ukraine would postpone its plans for NATO membership. Kellogg and Fleitz said that the plan was motivated by a concern that the war "has become a [war of attrition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_of_attrition) that's going to kill a whole generation of young men."[^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239]



Retired Lieutenant General [Keith Kellogg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keith_Kellogg) (pictured) and [Fred Fleitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frederick_H._Fleitz) presented a detailed plan to end Russia's war in Ukraine.

On 2 July, Hungarian prime minister [Viktor Orbán](750582.md) before proceeding to [visit Russia and China on 5 and 8 July](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_visits_by_Viktor_Orb%C3%A1n_to_Russia_and_China), respectively, self-appointing himself a mediator for ending the war.[^cite_note-HpURBCDW1720447101-240] In August, Ukraine began [an offensive into Russia's Kursk oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kursk_offensive_\(2024%E2%80%93present\)); Ukrainian presidential adviser [Mykhailo Podolyak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mykhailo_Podolyak) said one of the offensive's goals was to "inflict significant tactical defeats" to "convince the Russian Federation to enter into a fair negotiation process",[^cite_note-241][^cite_note-\:14-242] potentially to exchange Russian territory for [Russian-occupied Ukrainian territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian-occupied_territories_of_Ukraine) in a peace settlement.[^cite_note-\:14-242] The Kursk offensive disrupted plans for indirect talks in [Doha](26214389.md), Qatar, to halt [strikes on energy infrastructure in Ukraine](71963495.md) and [in Russia](70539619.md). Both countries reportedly planned to send delegations to talks mediated by Qatari officials, but Russian officials postponed the meeting after Ukraine's offensive.[^cite_note-washingtonpost-qatar-243] Ukrainian officials told *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* that the talks were postponed "due to [the situation in the Middle East](75895273.md)"[^cite_note-washingtonpost-qatar-243] but later declined to comment.[^cite_note-euractiv-qatar-244] Some officials had hoped the talks could be the first step toward a comprehensive peace deal.[^cite_note-washingtonpost-qatar-243] Putin said one week after the Kursk offensive began that he would not negotiate with Ukraine.[^cite_note-euractiv-qatar-244]

In September 2024, Zelenskyy proposed US participation in developing [Ukraine's natural resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_resources_of_Ukraine) in exchange for continued support in countering Russian aggression.[^cite_note-Briancon_20250226-245] Ukraine possesses significant deposits of minerals, including lithium, graphite, manganese, and titanium ore. Many of these resources remain unexploited, while some deposits are located in territories occupied by Russia.[^cite_note-Abdurasulov_202502263-246] The next month, Zelenskyy unveiled the [Victory Plan for Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victory_Plan_for_Ukraine),[^cite_note-247] which sought to solve to the conflict with Russia and describe future national defense guarantees.[^cite_note-248] The plan's official goal is "to change the circumstances in such a way that Russia will be forced to peace".[^cite_note-249] It outlined strategies to strengthen Ukraine and potentially end the war, including a renewed request for inclusion in NATO and a robust post-war security framework, assistance in deterring Russian aggression, and a "special agreement on joint investment and use" of Ukraine's natural resources—such as uranium, titanium, and lithium—with the [European Union](9317.md) and the United States.[^cite_note-Fornusek_20241016-250] After the plan was unveiled, French defense minister [Sébastien Lecornu](54364868.md)—under instruction from French president [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md)—entered into bilateral discussion with Ukraine that month to use the country's [rare-earth minerals](4697772.md) for France's defense industry.[^cite_note-Fornusek_20250227-251]

### 16th BRICS summit official statement

|  | This section **may contain material [unrelated to the topic of the article](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Guide_to_writing_better_articles#Stay_on_topic)**  and should be moved to *[16th BRICS summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/16th_BRICS_summit)* instead. Please help [improve this section](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Peace_negotiations_in_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)\&action=edit) or discuss this issue on the [talk page](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talk\:Peace_negotiations_in_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)#). *(December 2025)*_&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Maintenance_template_removal))_ |
