Participants of the [16th BRICS summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/16th_BRICS_summit) in [Kazan](202295.md), Russia. An official statement from the summit declared adherence to the [UN Charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Charter).

At the [16th BRICS Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/16th_BRICS_summit) in [Kazan](202295.md), Russia in October 2024, a formal declaration was issued declaring adherence to the [UN Charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Charter) and stating "appreciation \[for] relevant proposals of mediation and good offices, aimed at a peaceful resolution of the conflict through dialogue and diplomacy" in relation to the Russian invasion of Ukraine. The invasion of Ukraine was one of twelve topic areas that Reuters quoted from the summit's declaration.[^cite_note-252]

### Calls for renewed peace negotiations (November–December 2024)



US president-elect [Donald Trump](4848272.md) with French president [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) and Zelenskyy at the [Élysée Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89lys%C3%A9e_Palace) in Paris, December 2024

[Donald Trump](4848272.md) won the [November 2024 United States presidential election](59568546.md).[^cite_note-253] Trump had pledged during [his campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_2024_presidential_campaign) to negotiate an end to the war "within 24 hours" if elected[^cite_note-AP_Trump_Plans-254][^cite_note-NYT_NATO_Withdrawal-255]—though gave few details on how he would do so[^cite_note-\:0-256]—and had vowed to stop the "endless flow of American treasure to Ukraine".[^cite_note-AP_Trump_Plans-254][^cite_note-257] After winning the election, he began a push for peace negotiations between Russia and Ukraine.[^cite_note-258] On 7 December, as president-elect, Trump travelled to Paris for [the reopening](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reopening_of_Notre-Dame_de_Paris) of the [Notre-Dame de Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Notre-Dame_de_Paris), and met with both Macron and Zelenskyy to discuss the war,[^cite_note-259][^cite_note-260] and met with other European officials.[^cite_note-261][^cite_note-262]

Two days after Trump's election victory, [Sergei Shoigu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergei_Shoigu), Russia's [security council secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_of_the_Security_Council_of_Russia), said that the West should recognize Russia as winning the war and begin settlement negotiations.[^cite_note-\:0-256] Later that month, Zelenskyy proposed putting Ukraine-controlled territory "under the NATO umbrella" to "stop the hot stage of the war", and said that "Ukraine can get back the other part of its territory diplomatically."[^cite_note-263] In December, former NATO secretary general [Jens Stoltenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jens_Stoltenberg) said that Ukraine could temporarily cede Russian-occupied territory in exchange for peace.[^cite_note-264]

## 2025: developments during the second Trump presidency

### Beginning of Trump presidency

Further information: [February 2025 Putin–Trump phone call](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/February_2025_Putin%E2%80%93Trump_phone_call) and [61st Munich Security Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/61st_Munich_Security_Conference)

Trump assumed office as US president on 20 January 2025.[^cite_note-\:4-265] Two days later, he said he would impose further sanctions and [high tariffs](78448911.md) on Russia if Putin did not make a "deal" to end the war in Ukraine, though said he was "not looking to hurt Russia" and had "always had a very good relationship with President Putin".[^cite_note-MoscowTimesJan2025-266][^cite_note-267] Putin responded that he was ready to negotiate with Trump; Zelenskyy said Putin sought to "manipulate" the US president, though said he believed Putin would not succeed.[^cite_note-GuardianJan2025-268] Trump also said that Zelenskyy "shouldn't have allowed this war to happen", suggesting that Ukraine was partly responsible for the Russian invasion.[^cite_note-GuardianJan2025-268][^cite_note-269]



[Pete Hegseth](34076202.md) at a meeting of the [Ukraine Defense Contact Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Defense_Contact_Group) at [NATO headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO_headquarters), February 2025

On 12 February, during a meeting of the [Ukraine Defense Contact Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Defense_Contact_Group) at [NATO headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO_headquarters), Trump's defense secretary [Pete Hegseth](34076202.md) said that restoring Ukraine's pre-2014 borders was "an unrealistic objective" that would "only prolong the war". He also said that while Ukraine must have "robust security guarantees", the US "does not believe that NATO membership for Ukraine is a realistic outcome of a negotiated settlement."[^cite_note-270][^cite_note-nyt-20250212-271] Hegseth added that the US expected Europe to provide more financial and military assistance to Ukraine while the US focuses on China and its own security. He said that no US troops would be deployed as peacekeepers to Ukraine, and that any peacekeeping force should not be under NATO command.[^cite_note-nyt-20250212-271][^cite_note-guardian-20250212a-272]

Later that day, [Trump held a phone call with Putin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Putin%E2%80%93Trump_call) that he called "highly productive", and that they agreed to "have our respective teams start negotiations immediately" on an end to the war.[^cite_note-guardian-20250212b-273] Trump called Zelenskyy shortly after; Zelenskyy said that "Together with the US, we are charting our next steps to stop Russian aggression and ensure a lasting, reliable peace".[^cite_note-guardian-20250212b-273] Ukrainian and European officials were concerned by Trump unilaterally opening negotiations with Putin.[^cite_note-Reuters_13_Feb_25-274] Zelenskyy said Ukraine would not accept an agreement made without it and Ukrainian foreign minister [Andrii Sybiha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrii_Sybiha) said that "[Nothing can be discussed on Ukraine without Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nothing_about_Ukraine_without_Ukraine)", while the EU [foreign policy chief](750942.md), [Kaja Kallas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaja_Kallas), said that "Europe must have a central role" in peace negotiations[^cite_note-politico-20250212-275] and that any agreement made without Ukraine or the EU would fail.[^cite_note-Reuters_13_Feb_25-274] British defense secretary [John Healey](434625.md) also said that "There can be no negotiation about Ukraine without Ukraine."[^cite_note-Guardian_13_Feb_25-276]

Trump denied that he was excluding Zelenskyy from the peace process, but said that Ukraine "need\[s] to have elections too" and claimed that [favorability ratings of Zelenskyy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opinion_polling_for_the_next_Ukrainian_presidential_election) were "not great".[^cite_note-telegraph-20250212-277] In a joint statement, the UK, France, Germany, Italy, Poland, and Spain re-affirmed their support for Ukraine's "independence, sovereignty and territorial integrity" and said that "Ukraine and Europe must be part of any negotiations" and Ukraine must be given "strong security guarantees".[^cite_note-Guardian_13_Feb_25-276] French defense minister [Sébastien Lecornu](54364868.md) said the US seek "[peace through strength](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_through_strength)" rather than "peace through weakness", and German defense minister [Boris Pistorius](69097811.md) said the US should not have given Russia concessions before negotiations began.[^cite_note-Guardian_13_Feb_25-276] Russia said in a statement that it was "impressed" with Trump's position compared to that of Biden.[^cite_note-Reuters_13_Feb_25-274] The day after the UDCG meeting, Hegseth said that "everything is on the table" in peace negotiations, including Ukrainian membership in NATO and a negotiated return to its pre-2014 borders.[^cite_note-278] US vice president [JD Vance](53396477.md) added that the US could use "military tools of leverage" if Russia did not negotiate in [good faith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Good_faith_\(law\)).[^cite_note-279]



US and Ukrainian delegations at the [Munich Security Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/61st_Munich_Security_Conference)

Vance and Zelenskyy led a meeting of US and Ukrainian delegations at the [61st Munich Security Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/61st_Munich_Security_Conference), which ran from 14 to 16 February.[^cite_note-280] Zelenskyy also met with several [US senators](24909346.md) from both major US political parties.[^cite_note-tane25-281] [Vance's speech the MSC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_JD_Vance_speech_at_the_Munich_Security_Conference) reiterated a call for European leaders to contribute more to Ukraine's defense while the US focuses on China.[^cite_note-\:13-282] On 15 February, Trump's envoy [Keith Kellogg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keith_Kellogg) said that European countries would not be directly involved in US talks with Russia and Ukraine,[^cite_note-\:3-283] though the next day US secretary of state [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) said that both Ukraine and Europe would be part of any "real negotiations" to end the war,[^cite_note-284] and Trump's other envoy, [Steve Witkoff](40901053.md), rejected concerns that Ukraine or Europe would be excluded from peace talks.[^cite_note-285] NATO secretary general [Mark Rutte](4321056.md) said that European countries needed to create their own plan if they wished to play a major role in a peace settlement,[^cite_note-\:3-283][^cite_note-286] and Zelenskyy called for the creation of a unified [European army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_army) to challenge Russia.[^cite_note-287]

### US–Russia meeting in Saudi Arabia

Further information: [February 2025 United States–Russia meeting in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/February_2025_United_States%E2%80%93Russia_meeting_in_Saudi_Arabia)



US and Russian delegations at [a summit in Riyadh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/February_2025_United_States%E2%80%93Russia_meeting_in_Saudi_Arabia), February 2025

On 18 February, [American and Russian delegations met](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/February_2025_United_States%E2%80%93Russia_summit_in_Saudi_Arabia) in [Riyadh](51554.md), Saudi Arabia, for talks to develop a framework for further peace negotiations.[^cite_note-\:12-288] The US delegation was led by Rubio, joined by Witkoff and [Michael Waltz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Waltz), the US [national security advisor](44028.md).[^cite_note-\:52-289][^cite_note-\:02-290] The Russian delegation was led by foreign minister [Sergey Lavrov](541346.md) and presidential aide [Yuri Ushakov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuri_Ushakov).[^cite_note-\:43-291] The day prior, several European leaders met in Paris to discuss the war in Ukraine and Trump's peace efforts, including a proposal to send peacekeeping troops to Ukraine.[^cite_note-\:12-288][^cite_note-292] However, at the summit Lavrov rejected any NATO peacekeeping force and said that "the expansion of NATO, the absorption of Ukraine by the North Atlantic Alliance, is a direct threat to the interests" of Russia.[^cite_note-\:12-288] As a result of the summit, the US and Russia agreed to restore staffing at each other's embassies[^cite_note-\:52-289][^cite_note-\:62-293] and to explore the "economic and investment opportunities" that could arise as a result of a peace settlement in Ukraine.[^cite_note-\:12-288] China praised the peace talks.[^cite_note-294][^cite_note-295]

Trump said that he was "very disappointed" in Ukraine being "upset" about not being invited to the summit because it "had a seat for three years and a long time before that", and said he would not be opposed to European peacekeeping troops in Ukraine.[^cite_note-296] The summit was also intended to precede a future meeting between Putin and Trump,[^cite_note-297] which Rubio said would "largely depend on whether we can make progress on ending the war in Ukraine."[^cite_note-DW-298] Rubio said Trump was trying to discern whether Russia was serious about wanting an end to the war, and that "the only way" to find out was "to test them, to basically engage with them and say, 'Okay, are you serious about ending the war? And if so, what are your demands? Are your public demands and your private demands different?'"[^cite_note-DW-298] After the summit, Turkish president [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) said his country would be the "ideal host" for future talks between Russia, Ukraine, and the US, saying that both Russia and Ukraine view Turkey as a "reliable mediator".[^cite_note-299]

### Breakdown in US–Ukraine relations

Further information: [Ukraine–United States Mineral Resources Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine%E2%80%93United_States_Mineral_Resources_Agreement) and [2025 Trump–Zelenskyy Oval Office meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Trump%E2%80%93Zelenskyy_Oval_Office_meeting)



[Zelenskyy, Trump and Vance, 28 February](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Trump%E2%80%93Zelenskyy_Oval_Office_meeting)

After Zelenskyy's October 2024 "victory plan" introduced the idea of transactional opportunities with Ukraine's natural resources,[^cite_note-Fornusek_20250227-251][^cite_note-Fornusek_20241016-250] Trump said in early February 2025 that continued US support for Ukraine would be conditional on the US getting revenue from Ukraine's rare minerals.[^cite_note-Miller_20250203-300] Zelenskyy proposed US participation in developing Ukraine's natural resources in exchange for continued US support to defend against Russia.[^cite_note-Briancon_20250226-245] On 17 February, reports emerged that the [Trump administration](78298108.md) had asked that the US be given ownership of half of Ukraine's mineral and oil resources,[^cite_note-NBC_minerals-301] which Zelenskyy rejected reportedly because it did not offer Ukraine clear security guarantees.[^cite_note-NBC_minerals-301][^cite_note-302] Trump had also demanded that Ukraine pay the US US\$500 billion in compensation for US support during the war[^cite_note-Abdurasulov_202502263-246]—the US supplied Ukraine only US\$182 billion from 2022 to 2024[^cite_note-Hutzler_20250226-303]—and Trump later said that the US had contributed US\$350 billion to Ukraine.[^cite_note-Ricard_20250224-304]

On 18 February, Trump said that Ukraine "could have made a deal" to avoid the war and that they "should have never started it."[^cite_note-NBC_19_Feb_25-305][^cite_note-BBC_fact-check-306] The next day, Trump said that Ukraine should have [new presidential elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Next_Ukrainian_presidential_election)—elections scheduled for 2024 could not be held because [Ukraine's constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Ukraine) forbids elections during [martial law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martial_law_in_Ukraine)—and claimed Zelenskyy's approval ratings were only 4 percent;[^cite_note-BBC_fact-check-306] Zelenskyy replied by saying that Trump was living in a Russian "disinformation bubble".[^cite_note-NBC_19_Feb_25-305][^cite_note-Guardian_disinfo_bubble-307] Trump called Zelenskyy a "dictator without elections" on social media,[^cite_note-308][^cite_note-Reuters_20_Feb_25-309] and later said that he was negotiating "with no cards" for three years and "nothing got done".[^cite_note-310] Trump said that Russia did attack Ukraine, but that Zelenskyy and Biden were at fault for failing to prevent the invasion.[^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312] Trump's comments were criticized by US allies:[^cite_note-Reuters_20_Feb_25-309] Both Macron and British prime minister [Keir Starmer](18681491.md) said Zelenskyy was Ukraine's "democratically elected leader".[^cite_note-\:7-313][^cite_note-\:8-314] Macron said that this was "not the case for Vladimir Putin, who has been killing his opponents and manipulating his elections for a long time",[^cite_note-\:8-314] and Starmer added that it is "perfectly reasonable" for Ukraine to suspend elections during wartime.[^cite_note-\:7-313] German chancellor [Olaf Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz) called Trump's comments "false and dangerous".[^cite_note-Reuters_20_Feb_25-309]



Trump and Macron, 24 February



Trump and Starmer, 27 February

On 24 February, the [United Nations Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Security_Council) adopted the US-sponsored [UNSC Resolution 2774](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2774), which urged a lasting peace in Ukraine without a condemnation of Russia. Earlier, the [United Nations General Assembly](31957.md) adopted [a resolution condemning Russia for its invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-11/7) that the US voted against.[^cite_note-315] On 27 February, Trump extended a series of [sanctions against Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_sanctions_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War) over its invasion of Ukraine for one year.[^cite_note-316] Macron visited Trump in [Washington, D.C.](108956.md), on 24 February, during which he said that a truce between Russia and Ukraine could be agreed to in the coming weeks.[^cite_note-317] Trump said he wanted the war to end as quickly as possible and supported the presence of European peacekeepers in Ukraine. Macron said that a truce should be negotiated first, and that any subsequent peace agreement should include security guarantees for Ukraine.[^cite_note-318] Starmer [visited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_international_prime_ministerial_trips_made_by_Keir_Starmer) Trump on 27 February.[^cite_note-\:9-319] Starmer had previously said that he is "ready and willing" to put British troops on the ground in Ukraine if there is ceasefire agreement,[^cite_note-320] to ensure it is not "a temporary pause before Putin attacks again."[^cite_note-321] While Trump signaled approval of much of what Starmer had asked of him, he did not convince Trump to promise a US military backstop for a European-led peacekeeping force.[^cite_note-\:9-319]

Trump had said on 24 February that the US and Ukraine were close to an agreement "where we get our money back over a period of time."[^cite_note-Hutzler_20250226-303] On 25 February, negotiators for the two countries created a draft [Ukraine–United States mineral resources agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine%E2%80%93United_States_Mineral_Resources_Agreement).[^cite_note-Tarasova-Markina_20250226-322][^cite_note-CNN_fulltext_20250225-323] The draft agreement did not contain explicit future US security guarantees to Ukraine and would establish an investment fund for Ukraine's post-war reconstruction jointly owned by the two countries.[^cite_note-Tarasova-Markina_20250226-322][^cite_note-Butenko_20250226-324][^cite_note-Dutta_20250226-325] Zelenskyy called the deal a "framework" to be finalized during a 28 February in-person meeting with Trump.[^cite_note-Tarasova-Markina_20250226-322]



Zelenskyy and Trump during the 28 February meeting

[Zelenskyy met Trump on 28 February](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Trump%E2%80%93Zelenskyy_Oval_Office_meeting) in the [White House](33057.md). He sought to discuss the minerals agreement, push Trump to provide future US security guarantees for Ukraine, and restore their strained relationship.[^cite_note-326][^cite_note-Fornusek_20250227-251] The meeting was preceded by tensions between the Trump administration, which wanted Ukraine to make concessions to Russia to swiftly end the war, and the Zelenskyy government, which distrusted Russia's commitment to abide by agreements without US security guarantees.[^cite_note-327][^cite_note-\:22-328][^cite_note-329] During the press brief in the [Oval Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oval_Office), significant tension between Zelenskyy, Trump, and Vance arose. Vance said that diplomacy was the only way to end the war, to which Zelenskyy responded by saying that Putin had not respected previous ceasefires with Ukraine.[^cite_note-330][^cite_note-\:42-331] Trump intervened to tell Zelenskyy he was acting disrespectfully, accusing him of "gambling with World War III" and refusing peace talks.[^cite_note-\:42-331] The visit abruptly ended with Zelenskyy and the rest of the Ukrainian delegation being asked to leave the White House, while the minerals deal—for which Trump and Zelenskyy had scheduled a signing ceremony for later that day—went unsigned.[^cite_note-\:42-331][^cite_note-\:6-332]

### Trump–Zelenskyy meeting aftermath

Further information: [Coalition of the willing (Russo-Ukrainian war)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coalition_of_the_willing_\(Russo-Ukrainian_war\))

Three days after the Trump–Zelenskyy meeting, on 3 March the US suspended all military aid to Ukraine,[^cite_note-333] for which Trump cited dissatisfaction with Zelenskyy's commitment to peace negotiations, and the US government said the pause was necessary to ensure that US aid was "contributing to a solution" rather than prolonging the conflict.[^cite_note-334] On 5 March, the US halted intelligence sharing with Ukraine,[^cite_note-\:5-335] which US [Central Intelligence Agency](5183633.md) director [John Ratcliffe](42699041.md) suggested was temporary.[^cite_note-336] Zelenskyy said to the British media that he believes the relationship between the US and Ukraine can be restored and that he was ready to sign an improved Ukraine–US minerals agreement,[^cite_note-France24_20250303-337][^cite_note-Livingston_20250103-338] During [his speech to the US Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Donald_Trump_speech_to_a_joint_session_of_Congress) on 4 March, Trump addressed Zelenskyy with a more conciliatory message,[^cite_note-Swan_202503042-339] and mentioned that Zelenskyy was ready to sign the minerals deal and that he would ensure a quick settlement to the war.[^cite_note-Swan_202503042-339][^cite_note-340]

### London Summit on Ukraine



The [London Summit on Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_London_Summit_on_Ukraine), [Lancaster House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lancaster_House)

On 2 March, international leaders convened at the [London Summit on Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_London_Summit_on_Ukraine) hosted by the United Kingdom. Starmer had called the summit to draft a peace plan that could be brought to Trump, and to form a "[coalition of the willing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coalition_of_the_willing_\(Russo-Ukrainian_War\))" of European countries willing to take part in peace enforcement.[^cite_note-341][^cite_note-Deutsche_Welle_20250302-342] Leaders present at the London Summit were Starmer, Zelenskyy, Macron, the leaders of thirteen other EU member states, Canadian prime minister [Justin Trudeau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Trudeau), [European Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Council) president [António Costa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ant%C3%B3nio_Costa), [European Commission](9974.md) president [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md), and NATO secretary general Mark Rutte.[^cite_note-Hodunova_20250228-343][^cite_note-Le_Couteur_20250302-344][^cite_note-LeMonde_20250302-345] During the summit, Starmer and Macron proposed a one-month truce in Ukraine that could be made effective immediately, and during which negotiations could be held on a long-term settlement with security guarantees for Ukraine.[^cite_note-346]

### Thirty-day ceasefire proposal



US and Ukrainian delegations at a summit in Jeddah, March 2025

On 11 March, US and Ukrainian officials met in [Jeddah](277718.md), Saudi Arabia.[^cite_note-347] Ukraine accepted the US plan for a 30-day ceasefire,[^cite_note-\:10-348] which European leaders had proposed in London. As part of the plan, the Trump administration resumed all military aid and intelligence sharing with Ukraine, while Rubio said that a peace agreement now relied on Russia's approval of the ceasefire, saying "The ball is now in their court."[^cite_note-\:10-348][^cite_note-349][^cite_note-350] Pro-Kremlin Russian senator [Konstantin Kosachev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konstantin_Kosachev) responded that "Russia is advancing \[on the battlefield], so it will be different with Russia. Any agreements should be on our terms, not American."[^cite_note-351] Zelenskyy's chief of staff [Andriy Yermak](63086056.md) said after the Jeddah summit that "the key is now in Russia's hands. And the whole world will see who truly wants peace and who only talks about it."[^cite_note-352] Macron, Starmer and other European leaders welcomed the plan for a 30-day ceasefire.[^cite_note-353]

On 13 March, Putin stated there were issues which needed to be solved before Russia could agree to a ceasefire, effectively rejecting it.[^cite_note-354] Later the same day, US Envoy Witkoff met with Putin in Moscow.[^cite_note-355]

### Energy infrastructure ceasefire

On 18 March, following a phone conversation between Trump and Putin, Russia agreed to stop [attacking Ukrainian energy infrastructure](71963495.md) for one month,[^cite_note-356] with the ceasefire terms unclear.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-358]

Russian officials claimed that Russia abided by the ceasefire from 18 March, immediately after Putin's order. Ukrainian officials said the ceasefire began after the publication of the joint US–Ukrainian statement on 25 March.[^cite_note-\:16-359][^cite_note-\:17-360]

Both Ukraine and Russia accused each other of ceasefire breaches. Russia accused Ukraine of strikes on energy infrastructure in Kursk, Bryansk and Crimea during 25–26 March, though local governors did not report strikes. Ukraine accused Russia of striking civilian infrastructure almost every night since 18 March, with eight strikes against energy infrastructure, without detailing the damaged facilities.[^cite_note-\:16-359][^cite_note-\:17-360] The [ISW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_the_Study_of_War) reported on 27 March that Russia had intensified strikes against civilian non-energy infrastructure "in recent days", launching, for example, a ballistic missile and 86 drones on the night of 26–27 March.[^cite_note-361]

The energy infrastructure ceasefire ended on 18 April and was not renewed.

### 2025 Easter truce

On 19 April 2025, Putin announced Russia's intention to implement a 30-hour truce (from 6\:00 p.m. [MSK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moscow_Time) on 19 April to 11\:59 p.m. MSK on 20 April) to last through [Easter](9325.md); Ukraine immediately accepted this proposal. This was the first time ground hostilities were officially paused since the beginning of the invasion. Russia's offer was seen as a bid to appease Trump, who had demanded that some progress towards peace be shown by the end of April. Both sides accused each other of ceasefire breaches.[^cite_note-362]

### April 2025 US proposal

On 23 April 2025, the United States presented what it said was its final peace proposal. Under its terms, the US would acknowledge Crimea as *[de jure](43594.md)* part of Russia and would acknowledge Russian-occupied territory in Donetsk, Kherson, Luhansk, and Zaporizhzhia as *[de facto](43594.md)* part of Russia; there would be no Russian withdrawal except from the small Russian-held pockets outside of these regions; Ukraine would be barred from joining NATO but receive unspecified security guarantees; sanctions on Russia would be removed and US-Russian cooperation on energy and other industrial sectors resumed; and the US would operate the [Zaporizhia nuclear power plant](71700643.md) and use it to provide energy to both Russia and Ukraine. The proposal includes clauses that relate to economics and the rebuilding of Ukraine in addition to an agreement regarding mineral resources.[^cite_note-Reuters25-04-25-363]



Destruction in Kyiv after the [Russian attacks on 24 April](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2025_Russian_attack_on_Kyiv), which killed 13 civilians

Hours after the peace proposal was announced, Russia launched a [massive missile and drone attack on Kyiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2025_Russian_attack_on_Kyiv) and other regions, killing 13 Ukrainian civilians. It was the deadliest such attack in months. Trump responded on social media: "Not necessary, and very bad timing. Vladimir, STOP! 5,000 soldiers a week are dying. Lets get the Peace Deal DONE!". Neither Ukraine nor Russia accepted the proposal. Trump accused Zelenskyy of prolonging the "killing" for not accepting it. Ukraine's defense ministry said "These attacks are yet another confirmation—Russia is not seeking peace". The US president insisted that he was putting pressure on Putin, claiming that an agreement by Moscow not to take over the whole country would be a "pretty big concession".[^cite_note-364]

### May 2025 Istanbul negotiations



Ukrainian POWs released in a prisoner swap, 24 May 2025

On 15 May, Russian and Ukrainian delegations held direct talks in [Istanbul](3391396.md) for the first time since early 2022.[^cite_note-365]

Zelenskyy arrived in Turkey several days before and said he was ready for face-to-face talks with Putin. Trump suggested he would also be there. Zelenskyy said "There is no point in prolonging the killings. And I will be waiting for Putin in Turkiye ... I hope that this time the Russians will not look for excuses". He called for a ceasefire as a first step in negotiations. Putin rejected the call for a ceasefire[^cite_note-366] and turned down Zelenskyy's invitation to attend talks.[^cite_note-367] Trump excused Putin's absence, saying he believes Putin would only attend if *he* was there.[^cite_note-Conversation_May_25-368]

Russian and Ukrainian delegations exchanged their largely incompatible terms and only agreed on humanitarian issues such as exchange of prisoners and bodies. Russia again issued its maximalist demands that Ukraine give up all of the [provinces that Russia claimed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts) but had not fully occupied; which Ukraine had repeatedly rejected. Russia further demanded that Ukraine cut down its military significantly, not be part of any military alliance, not receive any Western military help, outlaw "nationalist parties and organizations", outlaw "[Nazi propaganda](70144390.md)", grant the [Russian language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language_in_Ukraine) official status, guarantee the rights of the Russian-speaking population, restore full diplomatic and economic ties with Russia, that neither side demand any [reparations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reparations_from_Russia_after_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War), and that all [international sanctions against Russia](43449324.md) be lifted.[^cite_note-369][^cite_note-370]

[Vladimir Medinsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Medinsky), who led the Russian delegation, said Russia was prepared to fight the war for as many years as was needed to achieve its goals.[^cite_note-371]

Russia's Security Council vice-chairman Dmitry Medvedev commented on 3 June that "The Istanbul talks are not for striking a compromise peace on someone else's delusional terms, but for ensuring our swift victory and the complete destruction of the neo-Nazi regime".[^cite_note-372]

Putin said on 27 June that Russian and Ukrainian terms remained "absolutely contradictory". He again rejected calls for an unconditional ceasefire and escalated attacks on Ukraine.[^cite_note-373]

### Trump deadlines



Apartment block in Kyiv destroyed in a Russian missile–drone attack on 17 June 2025, killing 28 civilians

[^cite_note-374]

On 8 May 2025, Trump warned that the US would impose further sanctions on Russia if it did not agree to a 30-day unconditional ceasefire, which Ukraine had agreed to.[^cite_note-375] Russia rejected the ceasefire call and continued to attack Ukraine.[^cite_note-376]

On 28 May 2025, Trump said he would know within two weeks whether Putin was serious about ending the war or was just "tapping us along".[^cite_note-377][^cite_note-\:0-256] In May, June and July, Russian missile and drone attacks on Ukraine increased dramatically.[^cite_note-378]

&#x20;On 1 June 2025, Zelenskyy said:

> We offered the Russians a ceasefire. Since March 11, the US proposal for a full and unconditional ceasefire has been on the table. It was the Russians who chose to continue the war—even under conditions where the entire world is calling for an end to the killing. And pressure is truly needed—pressure on Russia that should bring it back to reality. Pressure through sanctions. Pressure from our forces. Pressure through diplomacy.[^cite_note-379]

In June 2025, a majority of [US senators](70645797.md) supported [secondary sanctions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secondary_sanctions) against Russia to increase pressure on Putin, which would impose 500% [tariffs](78448911.md) on countries that buy [Russian oil](10450931.md), [natural gas](42827258.md), [uranium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mining_industry_of_Russia) and other exports. [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_and_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) and [India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_and_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) are the main consumers of Russian energy. Majority Leader [John Thune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Thune) said senators "stand ready to provide President Trump with any tools he needs to get Russia to finally come to the table in a real way."[^cite_note-380]

On 14 July 2025, Trump threatened to impose 100% tariffs and secondary sanctions on [countries purchasing Russian oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Russian_oil_products_sanctions_and_price_cap) if Russia did not agree to a ceasefire within 50 days.[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382] Trump later shortened the deadline to 8 August.[^cite_note-383]

### August 2025 Alaska summit

Main article: [2025 Russia–United States summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Russia%E2%80%93United_States_summit)



US president Donald Trump, Russian president Vladimir Putin and Russian Foreign Minister [Sergey Lavrov](541346.md) at the [summit meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Russia%E2%80%93United_States_summit) in [Alaska](624.md), August 2025

When Trump's deadline arrived on 8 August 2025, instead of imposing sanctions, Trump announced that he would host Putin in [Alaska](624.md) on 15 August to discuss a potential ceasefire.[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-Economist-385] It was the first time Putin had been invited to a Western country since launching the invasion. Shortly before the Alaska meeting, Trump warned that there would be "severe consequences" for Russia if Putin did not agree to a ceasefire.[^cite_note-386] In a joint statement, European leaders said: "We remain committed to the principle that international borders must not be changed by force. The current line of contact should be the starting point of negotiations," while saying that "the path to peace in Ukraine cannot be decided without Ukraine." At the time, the Trump administration was reportedly considering inviting Zelenskyy;[^cite_note-387] however, he was not invited.[^cite_note-Economist-385] The *[Financial Times](136566.md)* reported that, following Trump's sidelining of foreign policy experts, US experts on Russia "are not likely to be in the room" at the meeting.[^cite_note-388]

The Alaska summit ended without an agreement being announced. Despite warning of "severe consequences" if Putin did not agree to a ceasefire, Trump said after the meeting that there was no need for a ceasefire before a peace agreement. Trump also suggested that the onus was now on Ukraine to give up territory to end the war; he said he would support a plan to cede the whole [Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donbas) region to Russia (Russia controlled 88% of the Donbas region at the time).[^cite_note-f655-389][^cite_note-390] [Steve Witkoff](40901053.md) said that Putin agreed to let the US offer Ukraine NATO-like protection, which Zelenskyy welcomed.[^cite_note-391]

*[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* commented on the meeting that Putin had "ended his isolation in the West, made no public concessions, and can continue killing Ukrainians without further sanction."[^cite_note-392]

### August 2025 White House multilateral meeting

Main article: [August 2025 White House multilateral meeting on Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_2025_White_House_multilateral_meeting_on_Ukraine)



EU, NATO members and Zelenskyy in Washington D.C., 18 August 2025

On 18 August 2025, Trump and Zelenskyy, along with the leaders of the United Kingdom, Finland, France, Italy, Germany, NATO, and European Union, convened at the White House to discuss the aftermath of the 15 August summit. Robust security guarantees for Ukraine akin to [Article 5 of the North Atlantic Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Atlantic_Treaty#Article_5) were the primary topic on the agenda.[^cite_note-393][^cite_note-394][^cite_note-395] According to the [BBC](19344654.md), it was "unprecedented in modern times for so many world leaders to be \[at the White House] at once."[^cite_note-396] On 19 August 2025, Trump said in an interview with the Fox News, "When it comes to security, (Europeans) are willing to put people on the ground. We're willing to help them with things, especially, probably, ... by air."[^cite_note-397]

### Cancelled Budapest summit

Main article: [2025 Budapest Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Budapest_Summit)

On 16 October 2025, after a phone call from Putin that lasted over two hours, Trump announced he would meet Putin within the next two weeks in [Budapest](36787.md), [Hungary](13275.md), to discuss ending the war.[^cite_note-398][^cite_note-jg-399] On 20 October, the [Russian Foreign Ministry](4432213.md) said that US secretary of state [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) and Russian Foreign Minister [Sergey Lavrov](541346.md) held a call preparing for the summit.[^cite_note-400] Trump suggested that he would meet Putin and Zelenskyy separately at the summit.[^cite_note-401] Zelenskyy said he was willing to go to the summit and talk directly with Putin, asking "how can there be deals about us without us?".[^cite_note-402]

Five days after it was announced, the planned summit was cancelled.[^cite_note-jg-399] Trump suggested that it was called off because Putin refused a ceasefire and the Russians continued to push their maximalist demands.[^cite_note-\:24-403]

On 7 November, while hosting Hungarian prime minister [Viktor Orbán](750582.md) in the White House, Trump said that a summit in Budapest could still happen.[^cite_note-\:03-404] Later, Orban said the meeting had been postponed, not cancelled.[^cite_note-TASS_Budapest_still_agenda-405] On 11 November, Sergey Lavrov said the Russians were willing to have a meeting in Budapest "if and when the American colleagues return to their proposal".[^cite_note-TASS_Budapest_still_agenda-405]

Ukrainian president Zelenskyy travelled to Turkey again on 19 November in an attempt to revive negotiations with Russia on ending the war. US special envoy Steve Witkoff was due to join the talks. However, the Kremlin said that Russian representatives would not attend.[^cite_note-406]

### US 28-point plan

Wikisource logo

English [Wikisource](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikisource) has original text related to this article:&#x20;

**[Trump 28-point Ukraine–Russia plan (20 November 2025 version)](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/en\:Trump%2028-point%20Ukraine%E2%80%93Russia%20plan%20\(20%20November%202025%20version\))**



Jared Kushner, Steve Witkoff and Kirill Dmitriev in Russia, 2025

On 19 November 2025, *[Axios](52436164.md)* reported that [Steve Witkoff](40901053.md) had developed a draft 28-point plan with Trump's son-in-law [Jared Kushner](6191053.md) and Russian envoy [Kirill Dmitriev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirill_Dmitriev) in [Miami](53846.md) from 24 to 26 October.[^cite_note-407][^cite_note-economist-11232025-408] US officials said that Ukraine's national security chief [Rustem Umerov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rustem_Umerov) was also involved in drafting the plan. Umerov said his role was only "technical" and "I provided no assessments or, even more so, approvals of any points".[^cite_note-CBS_21-11-25-409]

The plan includes the following provisions:

- A ceasefire would be implemented, freezing the frontline as the *de facto* border in most areas and thus allowing Russia to retain control of Crimea, Luhansk, the [occupied parts of Donetsk](42425747.md), the [occupied parts of Zaporizhzhia](70396583.md), and the [occupied parts of Kherson](70396568.md), all of which would be recognized by the US and others as *de facto* part of Russia.

- Russian forces would withdraw from the pockets of territory that they occupy outside of these provinces.

- Ukrainian forces would withdraw from the part of Donetsk that they still hold; this area would become a neutral [demilitarized zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demilitarized_zone) without Russian military presence, but, alongside the rest of Donetsk, it would be recognized by the US and others as *de facto* part of Russia.

- Ukraine's military would be reduced in size and limited to 600,000 personnel while Russia's military would not be limited.

- Ukraine would be barred from ever joining NATO, and the alliance would not be allowed to take in new members. No NATO troops would be allowed in Ukraine—preventing the deployment of [European troops for peace enforcement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multinational_Force_%E2%80%93_Ukraine).

- The US would give a conditional security guarantee to Ukraine in return for payment:&#x20;

  - If Russia were to invade Ukraine again, the US would launch "a decisive coordinated military response", all sanctions would be reinstated, and Russia's conquests would no longer be recognized.

  - If Ukraine were to invade Russia, it would lose the security guarantee.

- Russia, Ukraine, and Europe would agree to a comprehensive [non-aggression pact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-aggression_pact).

- A committee would oversee the return of [POWs on both sides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prisoners_of_war_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine), [civilian detainees held by Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_filtration_camps_for_Ukrainians), and [abducted Ukrainian children](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_abductions_in_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War).

- Russian and Ukrainian leaders would have permanent [amnesty](18940624.md) for war crimes.

- [Zaporizhzhia Nuclear Power Plant](1763005.md) would be operated by the [IAEA](14984.md) and its electricity split equally between Russia and Ukraine.

- Russia and Ukraine would adopt [anti-discrimination laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-discrimination_laws).

- US sanctions on Russia would be gradually lifted, US\$100 billion in frozen Russian assets would be put into a new fund for joint Russian-American projects, and Russia would be invited to rejoin the [G8](59755.md).

- Ukraine would be rebuilt:&#x20;

  - US\$100 billion of frozen Russian assets would go toward US-led rebuilding and investment in Ukraine from which the US would receive 50% of the profits.

  - US\$100 billion of EU funds would go toward the rebuilding of Ukraine.

  - The US would help to rebuild, develop, and run Ukraine's gas infrastructure.

  - The Ukraine Development Fund would be formed to invest in fast-growing industries.

- [Ukraine would be eligible for EU membership](5612265.md) and have short-term preferential access to the European market before joining.

- Ukraine would hold elections within 100 days.

  [^cite_note-axios-20251119-410]

  [^cite_note-CBS_21-11-25-409]

  [^cite_note-AP_News_28-point_plan-411]

  [^cite_note-economist-11232025-408]

A senior US government official said that Trump had approved the plan, and that an Army delegation would present the plan in Ukraine.[^cite_note-nbc-20251119-412] On 19 November, a delegation led by US Secretary of the Army [Daniel P. Driscoll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_P._Driscoll) arrived in Ukraine to discuss the peace process. It also included US Army chief of staff General [Randy George](67617755.md), commander of US Army Europe-Africa General [Chris Donahue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Donahue), commander of [Security Assistance Group–Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_Assistance_Group%E2%80%93Ukraine) Lt. General [Curtis Buzzard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curtis_Buzzard), and Sergeant Major of the Army [Michael Weimer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Weimer).[^cite_note-413] Driscoll met Ukrainian defence minister [Denys Shmyhal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denys_Shmyhal) and President Zelensky. A US Army spokesman said the trip was a "fact finding mission to meet Ukrainian officials and discuss efforts to end the war". A Ukrainian official said talks would focus on the military situation.[^cite_note-bbc-20251119-414][^cite_note-axios-20251120-415] Driscoll reportedly told the Ukrainians that if a peace deal is not reached, Ukraine's defeat on the battlefield is imminent, and that Russia could continue the war indefinitely, while the US could not produce weapons and air defenses at the rate that Ukraine needs.[^cite_note-416]

#### Controversy over the plan

On 22 November, *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)*, among other media, reacted negatively to the plan.[^cite_note-economist-20251122-417] *The New York Times* described it as having "stunned" the diplomatic world, and that it was widely criticized as a capitulation to Russia.[^cite_note-418] Several US Senators said that Secretary of State Marco Rubio told them the plan was a Russian "wish list" and not the final plan, although the State Department denied the reports. The senators said the plan would reward Russia for its aggression and embolden other leaders who have threatened their neighbors.[^cite_note-419] *The Economist* reported that this exposed "confusion, rivalry, and incompetence within the Trump administration", and said the draft plan was likely leaked by Russia; although the *[BBC](19344654.md)* reported there were several possible sources for the leak.[^cite_note-economist-11232025-408][^cite_note-bbc-20251126-420]



European, Canadian and Japanese leaders meeting to discuss the US proposal during the [2025 G20 Johannesburg summit](78509872.md)

Ukrainian and European leaders viewed the original 28-point plan as capitulation to Russia.[^cite_note-AJE_why_euro_leaders_opposing-421] Thirteen European leaders, joined by Canadian and Japanese colleagues, met at the [2025 G20 Johannesburg summit](78509872.md) and agreed the "initial draft" needed "additional work".[^cite_note-422] They said the plan would leave Ukraine vulnerable to future attack, and went against the principle that borders must not be changed by force.[^cite_note-AJE_why_euro_leaders_opposing-421]

Trump responded that it was not his final offer to Ukraine[^cite_note-423] and accused Ukraine of showing "ZERO GRATITUDE".[^cite_note-economist-11232025-408] Trump said that Zelenskyy had until 27 November (the [Thanksgiving holiday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thanksgiving_\(United_States\)) in the US) to approve the plan, though US secretary of state Marco Rubio said there were issues and it could take longer to reach a deal.[^cite_note-reuters-20251123a-424]

Later in November, media reported a leaked audio recording of Witkoff advising his Russian counterparts on how to persuade Trump to accept the plan. Trump defended Witkoff, saying "That's a standard thing. He's gotta sell this to Ukraine, he's gotta sell Ukraine to Russia". In another leaked audio recording, [Kirill Dmitriev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirill_Dmitriev) said to [Yuri Ushakov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuri_Ushakov), "we'll just make this paper from our position, and I'll informally pass it along ... I don't think they'll take exactly our version, but at least it'll be as close to it as possible".[^cite_note-425] *Reuters* reported that the US plan drew from a Russian document given to the Trump administration in October.[^cite_note-426]

#### European revised plan

Wikisource logo

English [Wikisource](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikisource) has original text related to this article:&#x20;

**[Trump 28-point Ukraine–Russia plan (23 November 2025 version)](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/en\:Trump%2028-point%20Ukraine%E2%80%93Russia%20plan%20\(23%20November%202025%20version\))**

The UK, France and Germany issued a counter-proposal, following the structure of the US plan. The main changes from the US plan were:[^cite_note-reuters-20251123b-427][^cite_note-428][^cite_note-429]

- Negotiations about territory would only start *after a ceasefire*, and swaps of territory would be negotiated *starting from the frontline*;

- Ukraine could join NATO, but that would depend on consensus among NATO members, which at the time did not exist;

- NATO would agree not to *permanently* station troops under its command in Ukraine *in peacetime*

- The limit on Ukraine's military would rise to 800,000 personnel in peacetime, from 600,000 in the US plan;

- Russian assets will remain frozen until Russia pays reparations to Ukraine

- Ukraine will hold elections "as soon as possible" after the signing of the peace agreement, not "within 100 days" as in the US plan.

- Point 3 of US plan removed, which had stated "the expectation that Russia will not invade its neighbours and NATO will not expand further".

A 19-point version of the plan was negotiated in Geneva by Ukrainian, British, French, German and US representatives on 23 November. Any decisions falling under the EU's or NATO's remit would be discussed separately.[^cite_note-FT_US_UA_19point-430]

On 24 and 25 November there were meetings in [Abu Dhabi](18950756.md) between the US Secretary of the Army, Daniel Driscoll, and a Russian delegation to discuss the revised plan. A Ukrainian delegation was also present. US officials said that Ukraine had accepted the latest proposed plan, although some small details needed to be dealt with.[^cite_note-431][^cite_note-432]

On 25 November, a [Coalition of the Willing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coalition_of_the_willing_\(Russo-Ukrainian_war\)) meeting was held. Macron stated at the meeting that "very robust security guarantees and not paper guarantees" were a condition for peace; "real rock-solid guarantees are a necessity". Macron said in an interview that French, British or Turkish forces could be sent to Ukraine as peacekeepers.[^cite_note-KyivIndep_CoW_meets_agrees_robust-433]



Vladimir Putin, Steve Witkoff, Yuri Ushakov, and Kirill Dmitriev

On 26 November, Russian authorities stated that a preliminary agreement had been reached and that Witkoff and other US officials would go to Moscow the next week for more negotiations. Aboard [Air Force One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_Force_One), Trump said that Russia had agreed to some concessions.[^cite_note-434]



Kushner and Witkoff meeting with Putin in Moscow, 22 January 2026

On 2 December, US envoys [Jared Kushner](6191053.md) and Steve Witkoff traveled to Moscow and met for nearly five hours with Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md),[^cite_note-435] but the talks showed no "shift in Moscow's position".[^cite_note-436] On 8 December, Zelenskyy stated that Ukraine would not cede territory under pressure from either the US or Russia.[^cite_note-437]

#### Revised 20-point plan

Wikisource logo

English [Wikisource](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikisource) has original text related to this article:&#x20;

**[20-point Ukraine–Russia plan (24 December 2025 version)](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/en\:20-point%20Ukraine%E2%80%93Russia%20plan%20\(24%20December%202025%20version\))**

On 23[^cite_note-KyivIndep_Zel_reveals_full_20-438] and 24 December,[^cite_note-Reuters_20point_under_discussion-439] Zelenskyy published a 20-point plan that he said would be accompanied by a Ukraine–US–Europe security agreement, a Ukraine–US security agreement, and a Ukraine–US economic agreement.[^cite_note-KyivIndep_Zel_reveals_full_20-438] If the 20-point plan were accepted by Russian authorities, then, according to *[The Kyiv Independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Kyiv_Independent)*'s interpretation, a [ceasefire](592591.md) would be expected to start immediately after the plan is signed by Ukrainian, Russian, US and European leaders. The 20-point agreement would have to be [ratified](429650.md) by the [Ukrainian parliament](844993.md) or a [Ukrainian referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Referendums_in_Ukraine) in order to become formally effective.[^cite_note-KyivIndep_Zel_reveals_full_20-438]

On 11 February 2026, the Russian Foreign Minister [Sergey Lavrov](541346.md) effectively dismissed this 20-point plan, which had been expected to serve as the basis for peace negotiations.[^cite_note-\:27-440]

#### Analysis

Former Ukrainian foreign minister [Dmytro Kuleba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmytro_Kuleba) viewed the sequence of events surrounding the 28-point plan as following the same pattern as those around the February [2025 Trump–Zelenskyy Oval Office meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Trump%E2%80%93Zelenskyy_Oval_Office_meeting) and the August [2025 Russia–United States summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Russia%E2%80%93United_States_summit): a proposal is made by US officials favouring the Russian invasion; Ukrainian and European officials convince the US officials to consider a counter-proposal; and Russian forces increase their attacks on Ukraine. He recommended improved European and Ukrainian development of military defence capabilities.[^cite_note-Guardian_28pt_plan_dead_but-441]

Former US diplomat [Daniel Fried](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Fried) described the initial version of the 28-point plan as "a mess of Russian maximalist positions, contradictions, and ambiguity". He stated that a ceasefire and security for Ukraine were the two key elements needed for a viable peace deal, and that Putin would oppose both. He felt that a peace deal would be possible if Trump wanted to "get a peace deal right and \[were] prepared to push back on Putin".[^cite_note-KyivIndep_when_you_want_it_bad-442]

## 2026

### Trilateral meetings in Abu Dhabi

Main article: [2026 United States–Ukraine–Russia meetings in Abu Dhabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_United_States%E2%80%93Ukraine%E2%80%93Russia_meetings_in_Abu_Dhabi)

Trilateral meetings were held in [Abu Dhabi](18950756.md) between American, Russian and Ukrainian envoys, on 23 and 24 January 2026, with a second round of trilateral negotiations on 4 and 5 February also in Abu Dhabi.[^cite_note-443] One of Russia's demands was that Ukraine cede the parts of Donbas it still controlled to Russia, namely about 20% of the Donetsk province, as well as the rest of the Kherson and Zaporizhzhia provinces. This was a red line for Ukraine, thus these meetings ended without an agreement on the territorial issues.[^cite_note-444][^cite_note-\:20-445][^cite_note-\:18-446][^cite_note-447] Russia also demanded the international recognition of the annexed territories as part of Russia.[^cite_note-\:20-445] The [situation at the Zaporizhzhia Nuclear Power Plant](71700643.md) and who should control it was also discussed.[^cite_note-\:32-448][^cite_note-\:20-445] No further details of the talks were released to the press.

As the talks with Russia on stopping the war yielded no result and Russia insisted on the war to continue, the EU and the US prepared new sanctions against Russia. The EU prepared new sanctions against Russia's shadow oil tanker fleet, while the US had already sanctioned the Russian companies Rosneft and Lukoil in October 2025. Western sanctions against the Russian oil and gas had significant effects, with Russian oil and gas revenues falling 22% in the first eleven months of 2025.[^cite_note-\:20-445] On 6 February 2026, the EU announced its 20th package of sanctions against Russia, aimed at the Russian shadow oil tanker fleet, Russian energy, banks, goods and services, aswell as banks in other countries who cooperate with Russia to circumvent the sanctions.[^cite_note-449]

The talks ended with an agreement to exchange 314 prisoners of war, 157 from each side, which was the first prisoner exchange in five months.[^cite_note-\:20-445][^cite_note-\:44-450][^cite_note-451] As of September 2025, over 2,500 prisoners of war remained in Russian captivity.[^cite_note-\:23-452] Zelenskyy called this a "significant" result of the negotiations.[^cite_note-\:44-450]

Steve Witkoff said that "discussions will continue, with additional progress anticipated in the coming weeks".[^cite_note-\:23-452]

These were the first meetings between the three countries—the US, Russia and Ukraine—since the war started in February 2022.[^cite_note-453]

### Trilateral meetings in Geneva

Main article: [2026 United States–Ukraine–Russia meetings in Geneva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_United_States%E2%80%93Ukraine%E2%80%93Russia_meetings_in_Geneva)

Trilateral meetings between the US, Ukraine and Russia took place in [Geneva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva), Switzerland, on 17 and 18 February 2026. These talks followed a trilateral format similar to what took place in Abu Dhabi some weeks before. Zelenskyy said that territory would be the main issue to be discussed in the Geneva meetings.[^cite_note-\:19-454][^cite_note-\:21-455] The US proposed to set up a free economic zone in the parts of Donbas Russia did not control, namely 20% of the Donetsk province. The proposal was received with skepticism by both Ukraine and Russia. Zelenskyy wanted to submit the peace proposal to a parliamentary vote or a referendum, which could be held together with a presidential election if a ceasefire was in place and Ukraine had security guarantees.[^cite_note-\:19-454][^cite_note-\:21-455][^cite_note-456] On 14 February, at the [Munich Security Conference](82337810.md), Zelenskyy said that the US was offering security guarantees lasting 15 years as part of a peace deal, but Ukraine wanted them to last more than 20 years.[^cite_note-457]

These meetings were part of diplomatic efforts by the US to end the war by June 2026, who would likely pressure Ukraine and Russia to meet this deadline.[^cite_note-458][^cite_note-459] Though, Russian foreign minister [Sergey Lavrov](541346.md) said the negotiations were far from finished, rejecting what he called "over-enthusiastic perceptions" of progress, while claiming that the US had backtracked on the terms agreed upon at the [2025 Russia–United States summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Russia%E2%80%93United_States_summit), the so-called "spirit of [Anchorage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anchorage,_Alaska)".[^cite_note-460][^cite_note-461] Lavrov also effectively dismissed the [US-Ukraine 20-point peace plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_negotiations_in_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_\(2022-present\)#20-point_plan_and_draft_security_agreements), which had been expected to serve as the basis for peace negotiations.[^cite_note-\:27-440]

The appointment of [Vladimir Medinsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Medinsky) to lead the Russian delegation was met with dismay, given his history of uncompromising rhetoric in previous talks.[^cite_note-462] However, ahead of the meetings, US president Donald Trump urged Kyiv to move quickly toward an agreement. "Ukraine better come to the table, fast," Trump told reporters aboard Air Force One, appearing to suggest that the US and Russia "are in a position" to make a deal.[^cite_note-463][^cite_note-\:26-464]

The meeting on 17 February took place in the afternoon, after the US delegation met with an Iranian delegation, also in Geneva, to discuss Iran's nuclear program in the morning.[^cite_note-465] The leader of the Ukrainian delegation, [Rustem Umerov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rustem_Umerov), said the meeting was "focused on practical issues and the mechanics of possible solutions," and thanked the American delegation for "constructive cooperation".[^cite_note-\:110-466] A source close to the Russian delegation reported that the negotiations "were very tense", adding that they would, however, continue in the next day.[^cite_note-467]

A spokesperson for Zelenskyy said the talks had addressed Donbas and the fate of the Russian-controlled Zaporizhzhia Nuclear Power Plant, both sticking points since the beginning of the process.[^cite_note-468]

On the second day of talks, 18 February, the talks lasted less than two hours on the morning and ended "abruptly". They were described by the leader of the Russian delegation, Vladimir Medinsky, as "tough but businesslike", while the leader of the Ukrainian delegation, Rustem Umerov, said that "the talks were intensive and substantive".[^cite_note-469][^cite_note-\:33-470] President Zelenskyy said the agenda for the second day included humanitarian issues, particularly steps toward a prisoner of war exchange and the release of civilians.[^cite_note-\:45-471]

At the end of the meetings, Medinsky said a new round of negotiations would take place in the near future.[^cite_note-\:33-470] On 20 February, Zelenskyy announced that another round of trilateral meetings would take place within 10 days, likely also in Geneva.[^cite_note-472] Later, it was reported that the peace talks would likely take place in Abu Dhabi, but they were postponed due to the [Iran war](82533582.md).[^cite_note-473][^cite_note-474]

According to [*Axios*](52436164.md), progress in Geneva was limited. *Axios* reported that discussions among the military on mechanisms to monitor a ceasefire were more constructive, while the political negotiations remained "stuck", with the Russian envoys insisting on full control of the Donbas. In the interview with *Axios* published on 17 February, Zelenskyy said that any agreement requiring the Ukrainian forces to unilaterally withdraw from the remainder of the Donbas it still controlled, about 20% of the Donetsk province, and hand it over to Russia, would have to be approved in a national referendum, and that this would not happen.[^cite_note-475]

In an interview with [*Agence France-Press*](471981.md) on 20 February, Zelenskyy stated that both the US and Russia were pressing Ukraine to withdraw from the rest of the Donbas and cede the territory to Russia in exchange for a quick peace deal. Otherwise, Russia vowed to take the territory by force. He also said that he wished that a contingent of European troops to be sent to Ukraine once a ceasefire was reached, and that this contigent would be positioned close to the front line.[^cite_note-476]

An agreement to exchange prisoners of war was achieved at the trilateral meetings, and 500 prisoners from each side were exchanged on 5 and 6 March.[^cite_note-477][^cite_note-478][^cite_note-479]

### 2026 brief ceasefires

On 11 April 2026, a [32-hour ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2026_Russo-Ukrainian_truce) began for [Orthodox Easter](9325.md).

&#x20;Russia demanded a [48-hour ceasefire](83140694.md) beginning on 8 May 2026, so that it could mark [Victory Day on 9 May](337670.md). The Russian defense ministry threatened a "massive missile strike on the centre of Kyiv" if its truce demand was not met. In response, Zelenskyy announced an unlimited ceasefire from 6 May, saying Ukraine would observe the ceasefire for as long as Russia did. However, Russia continued attacking Ukraine.[^cite_note-480] Zelenskyy said:&#x20;

> They want Ukraine's permission to hold [their parade](83104217.md)—so they can safely take to [the square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Square) for an hour once a year, and then go back to killing our people and waging war. The Russians are already talking about strikes after May 9. A strange and certainly twisted logic from the Russian leadership.[^cite_note-481]

### Kazakhstan's proposal



Kazakh president [Kassym-Jomart Tokayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kassym-Jomart_Tokayev) and Russian president Vladimir Putin in Omsk, Russia, 25 July 2026

On 25 July 2026, during a meeting with Russian president Vladimir Putin in [Omsk](62593.md), Kazakh president [Kassym-Jomart Tokayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kassym-Jomart_Tokayev) called for the Russo-Ukrainian war to be “frozen” and for negotiations to resume on the basis of what he described as the “Istanbul Formula 2.0”. Tokayev argued that freezing the conflict could create conditions for renewed negotiations and suggested that a settlement could proceed under security guarantees from major powers, including Russia. He expressed concern over the [continued loss of life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casualties_of_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war) and said that the conflict should be brought to an end.[^cite_note-482][^cite_note-Reuters-2026-07-25-483]

Kremlin spokesman [Dmitry Peskov](9214683.md) subsequently said that freezing the conflict was impossible given Kyiv's position. He added that Russia's military operations could end if Kyiv made the “appropriate decisions” and reiterated that Russia's overriding condition was to achieve its stated objectives.[^cite_note-Reuters-2026-07-25-483]

## Overview of key negotiation points

Key negotiation points proposed or negotiated since the full-scale [Russian invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md) on 24 February 2022 include the following.

## Opinion polls

### Polls of Ukrainians

In a poll conducted by the [Kyiv International Institute of Sociology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyiv_International_Institute_of_Sociology) (KIIS) in May 2022, 82% of surveyed Ukrainians said they opposed any territorial concessions to Russia, even if that meant prolonging the war.[^cite_note-487] Another KIIS poll conducted in September 2022 found that 87% of surveyed Ukrainians opposed any territorial concessions to Russia.[^cite_note-488] A [Gallup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup,_Inc.) poll conducted in Ukraine in early September 2022 found that 70% of Ukrainians wanted to continue fighting until they achieve victory, while only 26% favored negotiations to end the war as soon as possible.[^cite_note-489] According to an opinion poll conducted in July 2022, 58% of Ukrainians said that [Crimea](163045.md) must be returned to Ukraine.[^cite_note-foreignpol230411-161]

In a survey of 1,200 Ukrainians conducted by KIIS in February 2024, 72% of respondents said that Ukraine should seek a diplomatic way to end the war in addition to a military solution.[^cite_note-490]

In March 2024, the [Carnegie Endowment for International Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnegie_Endowment_for_International_Peace) sponsored a survey of 2,000 Ukrainians in non-occupied Ukraine. Of the respondents:

- 43% supported peace negotiations with Russia, while 54% were against.

- 96% supported an end to the war whereby Russia withdraws from all occupied territories, including 94% of those who supported negotiations.

- 73% believed that Ukraine will eventually liberate all occupied territories.

- 65% opposed freezing the war at the current frontline, while 22% were in favor.

- 60% opposed giving up Ukraine's NATO membership bid as a concession, while 20% were in favor.

- 83% opposed limits on Ukraine's military as a concession.

- 86% believed that Russia will attack again even if there is a peace treaty, and 91% believed that Russia's motive for negotiations is to prepare for a new attack.

  [^cite_note-491]

  [^cite_note-492]

In a survey of Ukrainians conducted by the KIIS between 26 May and 1 June 2024, 58% of respondents said they opposed concessions in negotiations with Russia, down from 80% in May 2022.[^cite_note-493] In a survey by [ZN.ua media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dzerkalo_Tyzhnia) in July 2024, 44% of Ukrainians supported peace negotiations with Russia.[^cite_note-trump-ukraine-494]

[Gallup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup,_Inc.) opinion polls on whether Ukrainians want a negotiated end to the war as soon as possible, rather than fighting until Ukraine wins, have increased from 22% at the start of the war to 69% in 2025, with it becoming the majority opinion in late 2023.[^cite_note-telegraph-20250807-495][^cite_note-news-20250807-496]

### Polls of Russians

According to a survey conducted by the [Levada Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levada_Center) at the end of October 2022, 57% of Russian respondents favored the start of peace talks with Ukraine, and 36% preferred the continuation of hostilities.[^cite_note-497] The Kremlin's analysis concluded that public support in Russia for the war was broad but not deep, and that most Russians would accept anything Putin would call a victory. In September 2023, the head of the [VTsIOM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Public_Opinion_Research_Center) state pollster Valery Fyodorov said in an interview that only 10-15% of Russians actively supported the war, and that "most Russians are not demanding the conquest of Kyiv or Odesa."[^cite_note-NYTimes-2023-12-23-208]

### Polls in other countries

| --- | --- |

A poll of Germans conducted by the [Forsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forsa_Institute) and published in January 2023 found that over 80% believe it is more important to end the war through negotiations than for Ukraine to win, with only 18% disagreeing.[^cite_note-498] A [YouGov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouGov) poll showed that in February 2023, 63% of respondents in Sweden wanted to support Ukraine in a war with Russia until Russian troops leave all occupied territories, even if it means prolonging the war. The corresponding figures were: in Denmark, 56%; in Britain 53%; in the United States 46%; in Spain 44%; Germany 40%; France 37% and Italy 29%.[^cite_note-499]

A US poll in May 2023 showed 46% public support for arming Ukraine in its war against Russia. By October 2023, this support had dropped to 41%. Both sides of the political spectrum saw a decline. Since the counteroffensive started in June 2023, Ukrainian forces have only retaken a series of small villages and settlements and are only in control of a small percentage of the territories occupied by Russian forces during the conflict.[^cite_note-500]
